- **ComponentStore** and **`updater`**: the RxJS-based local store of `@ngrx/component-store`, a class you extend (`setState`, `select`, `effect`), and a pure `(state, value) => state` function exposed as a method ([28 §8](../modules/28-state-management.md#8-componentstore-and-signalstore-signalstate-signalstore-withstate-withcomputed-withmethods-and-protected-state))
- **SignalStore** and **store feature**: the signal-based store of `@ngrx/signals`, an injectable class that `signalStore(...)` assembles from functions (`withState`, `withComputed`, `withMethods`, `withHooks`, `withProps`) that each add members ([28 §8](../modules/28-state-management.md#8-componentstore-and-signalstore-signalstate-signalstore-withstate-withcomputed-withmethods-and-protected-state))
- **`signalState`**: the state container without a class or features, a plain object of signals ([28 §8](../modules/28-state-management.md#8-componentstore-and-signalstore-signalstate-signalstore-withstate-withcomputed-withmethods-and-protected-state))
- **deep signal**: the signal `withState` makes for a nested object; it also exposes one signal per property ([28 §8](../modules/28-state-management.md#8-componentstore-and-signalstore-signalstate-signalstore-withstate-withcomputed-withmethods-and-protected-state))
- **`patchState`**: the function that merges a partial state into a store or `signalState` ([28 §8](../modules/28-state-management.md#8-componentstore-and-signalstore-signalstate-signalstore-withstate-withcomputed-withmethods-and-protected-state))
- **protected state** and **`unprotected`**: the type-level rule that only a store's own methods may call `patchState`, switched off by `protectedState: false`, and the `@ngrx/signals/testing` helper that lets a test write ([28 §8](../modules/28-state-management.md#8-componentstore-and-signalstore-signalstate-signalstore-withstate-withcomputed-withmethods-and-protected-state))
- **`rxMethod`** and **`signalMethod`**: functions that turn an RxJS pipeline (or a plain function) into a store method callable with a value, a signal or, for `rxMethod`, an Observable ([28 §9](../modules/28-state-management.md#9-signalstore-in-depth-rxmethod-withentities-custom-features-and-the-events-plugin))
- **`withEntities`**, **`entityMap`** and **entity updater**: the store feature that adds `ids`, `entityMap` and a computed `entities`, the lookup of entities by id, and a pure function such as `addEntity` passed to `patchState` ([28 §9](../modules/28-state-management.md#9-signalstore-in-depth-rxmethod-withentities-custom-features-and-the-events-plugin))
- **custom feature**, **`signalStoreFeature`** and **`type<>()`**: a reusable bundle of store features, the function that builds it, and the compile-time declaration of members the host store must have ([28 §9](../modules/28-state-management.md#9-signalstore-in-depth-rxmethod-withentities-custom-features-and-the-events-plugin))
- **Events plugin**, **event**, **`eventGroup`** and **dispatcher**: the Flux-style layer of `@ngrx/signals/events`, a `{ type, payload }` object, the creator of a family of events typed `[Source] name`, and the bus that delivers them ([28 §9](../modules/28-state-management.md#9-signalstore-in-depth-rxmethod-withentities-custom-features-and-the-events-plugin))
- **`withReducer`**, **`withEventHandlers`** and **`injectDispatch`**: the features that turn events into state changes and into side effects, and the function that returns dispatching functions ([28 §9](../modules/28-state-management.md#9-signalstore-in-depth-rxmethod-withentities-custom-features-and-the-events-plugin))
- **optimistic update**, **pessimistic update**, **rollback** and **inverse operation**: applying a change locally before the server answers, waiting for the answer first, undoing the change when the server fails, and the previous value or call that undoes it ([28 §10](../modules/28-state-management.md#10-optimistic-updates-normalization-and-undo))
- **normalization** and **denormalization**: storing a collection as `ids` plus an id-keyed map so one update replaces one entry, and joining them back for display ([28 §10](../modules/28-state-management.md#10-optimistic-updates-normalization-and-undo))
- **undo stack**, **redo stack** and **history bound**: the `past` and `future` stacks around the `present`, and the cap on how many past values are kept ([28 §10](../modules/28-state-management.md#10-optimistic-updates-normalization-and-undo))
- **decision framework** and **traceability**: a fixed list of questions that picks a state tool from facts about the state, and a record of which change produced which state ([28 §11](../modules/28-state-management.md#11-alternatives-and-the-decision-framework-ngxs-elf-tanstack-query-and-when-a-store-library-is-justified))
- **server-cache library**: a library that fetches, caches by key and refreshes server data, such as TanStack Query ([28 §11](../modules/28-state-management.md#11-alternatives-and-the-decision-framework-ngxs-elf-tanstack-query-and-when-a-store-library-is-justified))
- **query key**: the array that names a TanStack Query cache entry ([28 §11](../modules/28-state-management.md#11-alternatives-and-the-decision-framework-ngxs-elf-tanstack-query-and-when-a-store-library-is-justified))
- **stale-while-revalidate**: serving the cached copy while fetching a fresh one ([28 §11](../modules/28-state-management.md#11-alternatives-and-the-decision-framework-ngxs-elf-tanstack-query-and-when-a-store-library-is-justified))
- **mutation** (TanStack Query): a server write run by `injectMutation`, which tracks its pending and error state ([28 §11](../modules/28-state-management.md#11-alternatives-and-the-decision-framework-ngxs-elf-tanstack-query-and-when-a-store-library-is-justified))
- **NGXS state class**: a class decorated with `@State` whose `@Action` methods change the state through a `StateContext` ([28 §11](../modules/28-state-management.md#11-alternatives-and-the-decision-framework-ngxs-elf-tanstack-query-and-when-a-store-library-is-justified))
- **Elf store**: the store `createStore` returns in `@ngneat/elf`, a `BehaviorSubject` with `update(...reducers)` ([28 §11](../modules/28-state-management.md#11-alternatives-and-the-decision-framework-ngxs-elf-tanstack-query-and-when-a-store-library-is-justified))
- **`ActionsSubject`**, **`ReducerManager`** and **`State`**: the `BehaviorSubject` of dispatched actions, the holder of the combined reducer, and the class that applies the reducer to each action with `scan` ([28 §12](../modules/28-state-management.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ngrx-store-reduces-actions-and-how-signalstore-keeps-state-in-signals))
- **queue scheduler** (in NgRx Store): the scheduler `State` observes actions on, which runs a task at once unless another is running and then queues it ([28 §12](../modules/28-state-management.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ngrx-store-reduces-actions-and-how-signalstore-keeps-state-in-signals))
- **`STATE_SOURCE`** and **state watcher**: SignalStore's internal symbol holding one `WritableSignal` per top-level key, and a `watchState` callback that receives the whole state after each `patchState` ([28 §12](../modules/28-state-management.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ngrx-store-reduces-actions-and-how-signalstore-keeps-state-in-signals))

## Module 29

- **test level**: how much real code a test runs and what it fakes; unit, component, integration and end-to-end are the usual levels ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **unit test**: a test that runs one function or class with its collaborators replaced by test doubles ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **component test**: a test that renders one component's template in `TestBed` on a DOM such as jsdom ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **integration test**: a test that runs several real units together (a routed page, its service, its HTTP calls) and fakes only the network ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **end-to-end test**: a test that drives the built app in a real browser against a running server; module 30 owns it ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **static analysis**: checking code without running it, such as the type checker and the linter ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **test double**: a stand-in for a collaborator in a test (stub, fake, spy, mock) ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **testing pyramid**: the test-mix shape credited to Mike Cohn: many low-level unit tests, fewer service tests, few UI tests ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **testing trophy**: Kent C. Dodds's test-mix shape: static analysis at the base, then unit, most effort in integration, few end-to-end ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **deep test**: a component test that renders the component with its real children ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **shallow test**: a component test that replaces children with stubs, for example through `TestBed.overrideComponent` ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **confidence** (of a test): how strongly a passing test predicts working software ([29 §1](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#1-what-to-test-where-unit-integration-component-and-end-to-end-tests-the-pyramid-and-the-trophy))
- **builder**: the CLI unit behind an `angular.json` target such as `test`, here `@angular/build:unit-test` ([29 §2](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#2-the-runner-vitest-through-angularbuildunit-test-jsdom-and-browser-mode))
- **virtual file**: a module that exists only inside the build, such as the unit-test builder's init files ([29 §2](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#2-the-runner-vitest-through-angularbuildunit-test-jsdom-and-browser-mode))
- **test runner**: the tool that finds `describe` and `it`, runs them and reports; here Vitest ([29 §2](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#2-the-runner-vitest-through-angularbuildunit-test-jsdom-and-browser-mode))
- **jsdom**: a JavaScript implementation of the DOM for Node, with no layout or rendering ([29 §2](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#2-the-runner-vitest-through-angularbuildunit-test-jsdom-and-browser-mode))
- **browser provider**: a package that lets Vitest drive a real browser (browser mode) ([29 §2](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#2-the-runner-vitest-through-angularbuildunit-test-jsdom-and-browser-mode))
- **watch mode**: a run that repeats the affected specs on every file change ([29 §2](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#2-the-runner-vitest-through-angularbuildunit-test-jsdom-and-browser-mode))
- **coverage**: a report of which source lines the tests executed ([29 §2](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#2-the-runner-vitest-through-angularbuildunit-test-jsdom-and-browser-mode))
- **Karma**: a test runner that launches real browsers and runs Jasmine specs in them; the Angular CLI default before v21 ([29 §3](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#3-karma-jasmine-and-jest-the-legacy-and-alternative-runners-and-migrating-to-vitest))
- **Jasmine**: a test framework with its own `describe`, `it`, `expect` and spies, used with Karma ([29 §3](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#3-karma-jasmine-and-jest-the-legacy-and-alternative-runners-and-migrating-to-vitest))
- **Jest**: a Node test runner with jsdom that Angular projects use through the third-party `jest-preset-angular` ([29 §3](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#3-karma-jasmine-and-jest-the-legacy-and-alternative-runners-and-migrating-to-vitest))
- **spy object**: a stand-in whose methods are all spies; Jasmine builds one with `createSpyObj`, the migration writes an object of `vi.fn()` calls ([29 §3](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#3-karma-jasmine-and-jest-the-legacy-and-alternative-runners-and-migrating-to-vitest))
- **schematic**: a generator the Angular CLI runs to create or rewrite files, such as `refactor-jasmine-vitest` ([29 §3](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#3-karma-jasmine-and-jest-the-legacy-and-alternative-runners-and-migrating-to-vitest))
- **ng update migration**: a code change the CLI applies to a workspace when updating a package, such as `migrate-karma-to-vitest` ([29 §3](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#3-karma-jasmine-and-jest-the-legacy-and-alternative-runners-and-migrating-to-vitest))
- **testing module**: the Angular module `TestBed` builds for one test from its configured providers and imports; it is rebuilt before each test ([29 §4](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#4-testbed-the-testing-module-inject-runininjectioncontext-overrides-and-teardown))
- **instantiation** (of the testing module): the first `TestBed.inject` or `createComponent` call, which creates the module's injector and locks its configuration ([29 §4](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#4-testbed-the-testing-module-inject-runininjectioncontext-overrides-and-teardown))
- **teardown (TestBed)**: destroying the testing module and its fixtures after each test ([29 §4](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#4-testbed-the-testing-module-inject-runininjectioncontext-overrides-and-teardown))
- **override** (in `TestBed`): a test-only replacement of a provider or of metadata, applied before the module is instantiated ([29 §4](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#4-testbed-the-testing-module-inject-runininjectioncontext-overrides-and-teardown))
- **`PlatformLocation`**: the service the router reads the URL through; `TestBed` provides a fake one ([29 §4](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#4-testbed-the-testing-module-inject-runininjectioncontext-overrides-and-teardown))
- **fixture** (`ComponentFixture`): the test's handle on one component rendered by `TestBed`, with its element, instance and change-detection controls ([29 §5](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#5-zoneless-fixtures-whenstable-testbedtick-detectchanges-and-automatic-change-detection))
- **auto-detect** (of a fixture): the fixture renders by itself, like the app, when something notifies the scheduler; always on in a zoneless test ([29 §5](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#5-zoneless-fixtures-whenstable-testbedtick-detectchanges-and-automatic-change-detection))
- **stable** (fixture): no pending task is left, so scheduled work has finished; `await fixture.whenStable()` waits for it ([29 §5](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#5-zoneless-fixtures-whenstable-testbedtick-detectchanges-and-automatic-change-detection))
- **tick**: one change-detection pass of the whole application; `TestBed.tick()` forces one in a test ([29 §5](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#5-zoneless-fixtures-whenstable-testbedtick-detectchanges-and-automatic-change-detection))
- **host component**: a small test-only component whose template contains the component under test, used when the template contract (projection, bindings in markup) is what the test checks ([29 §6](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#6-component-tests-dom-queries-componentfixture-signal-inputs-with-setinput-and-bindings-outputs-and-onpush))
- **`DebugElement`**: Angular's wrapper around a rendered node, with `query`, `queryAll`, `listeners` and `triggerEventHandler` ([29 §6](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#6-component-tests-dom-queries-componentfixture-signal-inputs-with-setinput-and-bindings-outputs-and-onpush))
- **`By.css`**: a predicate builder for `DebugElement.query` that matches nodes by CSS selector ([29 §6](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#6-component-tests-dom-queries-componentfixture-signal-inputs-with-setinput-and-bindings-outputs-and-onpush))
- **`setInput`**: `ComponentRef.setInput(name, value)`, which sets a component input from outside the template and schedules a render ([29 §6](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#6-component-tests-dom-queries-componentfixture-signal-inputs-with-setinput-and-bindings-outputs-and-onpush))
- **`bindings`** (of `TestBed.createComponent`): an option that takes binding functions such as `inputBinding` and `outputBinding` to feed inputs and listen to outputs ([29 §6](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#6-component-tests-dom-queries-componentfixture-signal-inputs-with-setinput-and-bindings-outputs-and-onpush))
- **`OutputEmitterRef`**: the object `output()` returns; a test observes it with `subscribe` ([29 §6](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#6-component-tests-dom-queries-componentfixture-signal-inputs-with-setinput-and-bindings-outputs-and-onpush))
- **accessible name**: the text assistive technology announces for a control; a query by it tests the page the way a user meets it ([29 §6](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#6-component-tests-dom-queries-componentfixture-signal-inputs-with-setinput-and-bindings-outputs-and-onpush))
- **dummy**: a test double passed around but never used, usually to fill a parameter list ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **stub**: a test double that gives canned answers to calls made during the test ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **spy**: a stub that also records how it was called; `vi.fn()` and `vi.spyOn` create spies ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **mock**: a test double pre-programmed with expectations about the calls it should receive; not the same as `vi.fn` ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **fake**: a test double with a working implementation that takes a shortcut, such as an in-memory store ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **seam**: the place where one implementation can be swapped for another; in Angular, dependency injection ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **port**: the interface or `InjectionToken` that names what a unit needs from a collaborator, so a double can implement it ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **over-mocking**: replacing so much, or an invented contract, that a test passes while the real code is broken or the test pins how the code works ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **`vi.fn`**: Vitest's factory for a spy that can also be given canned answers; records calls in `mock.calls` ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **`vi.spyOn`**: Vitest's way to spy on an existing method, keeping the original implementation until overridden; undone by `mockRestore` or `vi.restoreAllMocks` ([29 §7](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#7-test-doubles-fakes-stubs-spies-and-mocks-vifn-provider-overrides-and-over-mocking))
- **host element** (of a directive test): the element a directive is attached to in a test; `TestBed.createDirective` renders one with the tag name it is given ([29 §8](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#8-testing-services-pipes-and-directives-including-testbedcreatedirective))
- **`DirectiveFixture`**: the fixture `TestBed.createDirective` returns; it adds `directiveInstance` and `onDestroy` to the usual fixture members ([29 §8](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#8-testing-services-pipes-and-directives-including-testbedcreatedirective))
- **`TestBed.createDirective`**: renders a directive on a host element of a chosen tag and returns a `DirectiveFixture`; added in Angular 22.2 ([29 §8](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#8-testing-services-pipes-and-directives-including-testbedcreatedirective))
- **`UrlTree`**: the router's parsed, immutable representation of a URL; a guard returns one to redirect, and `String(urlTree)` prints it ([29 §9](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#9-testing-guards-interceptors-forms-effects-resources-and-stores))
- **`RouterTestingHarness`**: the router's test helper; it renders a root `router-outlet`, navigates with `navigateByUrl` and waits for the navigation and its render ([29 §9](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#9-testing-guards-interceptors-forms-effects-resources-and-stores))
- **testing backend**: the `HttpClient` backend that `provideHttpClientTesting()` installs, whose requests an `HttpTestingController` inspects and flushes instead of reaching the network ([29 §9](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#9-testing-guards-interceptors-forms-effects-resources-and-stores))
- **`MockStore`**: the NgRx test double that holds a state you set and lets a test override selector results; `provideMockStore` registers it ([29 §9](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#9-testing-guards-interceptors-forms-effects-resources-and-stores))
- **mock clock**: a replacement for the timer functions that moves only when the test advances it ([29 §10](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#10-async-tests-vitest-fake-timers-versus-fakeasync-and-waitforasync))
- **fake timers**: Vitest's mock clock, installed with `vi.useFakeTimers()` and removed with `vi.useRealTimers()` ([29 §10](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#10-async-tests-vitest-fake-timers-versus-fakeasync-and-waitforasync))
- **ProxyZone**: the zone.js zone that records pending work for a test; `waitForAsync` needs it and the Vitest patch provides it ([29 §10](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#10-async-tests-vitest-fake-timers-versus-fakeasync-and-waitforasync))
- **component harness**: a class, written once per component, that exposes intent methods and hides the component's DOM from tests ([29 §11](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#11-component-harnesses-and-angular-testing-library))
- **harness loader**: the object that finds harnesses under a root element; `TestbedHarnessEnvironment.loader(fixture)` creates one ([29 §11](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#11-component-harnesses-and-angular-testing-library))
- **harness predicate**: a `HarnessPredicate` that narrows a harness query by options such as a label ([29 §11](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#11-component-harnesses-and-angular-testing-library))
- **`TestElement`**: the harness's async handle on one DOM node; each action awaits fixture stability ([29 §11](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#11-component-harnesses-and-angular-testing-library))
- **Angular Testing Library**: `@testing-library/angular`, which renders a component through `TestBed` and queries the page like a user ([29 §11](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#11-component-harnesses-and-angular-testing-library))
- **role query**: a Testing Library query such as `getByRole('button', { name })` that finds a node by role and accessible name ([29 §11](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#11-component-harnesses-and-angular-testing-library))
- **driver**: the object a test drives a component through, such as a harness or the rendered page ([29 §11](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#11-component-harnesses-and-angular-testing-library))
- **worker** (Vitest): a thread Vitest runs spec files in; the unit-test builder's init file sets up `TestBed` once in it ([29 §12](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#12-under-the-hood-how-the-builder-boots-testbed-and-how-a-fixture-decides-it-is-stable))
- **test environment** (`TestBed`): the base module, platform and options that `initTestEnvironment` sets once and every testing module extends ([29 §12](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#12-under-the-hood-how-the-builder-boots-testbed-and-how-a-fixture-decides-it-is-stable))
- **cleanup hook**: the function `@angular/core/testing` exports for the runner's `beforeEach` and `afterEach`; it resets the testing module ([29 §12](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#12-under-the-hood-how-the-builder-boots-testbed-and-how-a-fixture-decides-it-is-stable))
- **test `ApplicationRef`**: the testing module's application reference, which also tracks the views of test fixtures ([29 §12](../modules/29-testing-angular.md#12-under-the-hood-how-the-builder-boots-testbed-and-how-a-fixture-decides-it-is-stable))

## Module 30

- **end-to-end (e2e) test**: a test that drives the built app in a real browser against a running server and checks a user journey ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **user journey**: a path through the app that delivers one outcome, such as adding a todo and finding it after a reload ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **production build**: the optimized, hash-named files `ng build` writes with the production configuration, the artifact that ships ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **risk** (testing): one way the app can break, which a strategy assigns to a test level ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **risk-based test selection**: assigning each risk to the cheapest test level that can catch it ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **SPA fallback**: a server rule that answers app routes with `index.html` so the client router can handle deep links ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **browser context**: an isolated browser profile with its own cookies and storage; Playwright gives each test a fresh one ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **smoke test**: a test that only checks that the built app starts and its main page loads ([30 §1](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#1-what-belongs-at-the-end-to-end-level-journeys-cost-and-a-strategy-across-levels))
- **Playwright**: a browser automation library and test runner whose test code runs in Node and drives browsers out of process ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **Cypress**: an end-to-end test runner whose test code runs inside the browser, beside the app ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **worker process** (Playwright): an OS process the test runner starts to run test files; each starts its own browser ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **builder**: the function an `angular.json` target names; `ng e2e` runs the one registered as the `e2e` target ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **`ng add`**: the CLI command that installs a package and runs its setup schematic ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **superdomain** (Cypress): a shared parent domain; Cypress binds each test to one ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **Protractor**: the Angular team's former end-to-end framework, built on WebDriverJS; its CLI builder was removed in v19.0 ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **WebDriver**: the W3C remote control interface for introspecting and controlling browsers ([30 §2](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#2-playwright-and-cypress-architecture-ng-e2e-and-the-end-of-protractor))
- **actionability**: the set of checks an element must pass before Playwright performs an action on it (exactly one match, visible, stable, receives events, enabled) ([30 §3](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#3-auto-waiting-actionability-checks-web-first-assertions-and-cypress-retry-ability))
- **auto-waiting**: Playwright retrying the actionability checks until they pass or the action's timeout ends ([30 §3](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#3-auto-waiting-actionability-checks-web-first-assertions-and-cypress-retry-ability))
- **web-first assertion**: an `expect(locator)` matcher that re-fetches the element and retries until it passes or the `expect` timeout (5 s) ends ([30 §3](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#3-auto-waiting-actionability-checks-web-first-assertions-and-cypress-retry-ability))
- **retry-ability** (Cypress): Cypress's rule that queries and assertions are retried until a timeout while other commands run once ([30 §3](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#3-auto-waiting-actionability-checks-web-first-assertions-and-cypress-retry-ability))
- **`TimeoutError`** (Playwright): the error an action or assertion throws when its wait ends before the condition holds ([30 §3](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#3-auto-waiting-actionability-checks-web-first-assertions-and-cypress-retry-ability))
- **locator (Playwright)**: a description of how a user finds an element, resolved again on every use and strict by default ([30 §4](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#4-locators-page-objects-and-fixtures))
- **accessible name**: the text assistive technology announces for a control, which `getByRole` matches with its `name` option ([30 §4](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#4-locators-page-objects-and-fixtures))
- **strict mode (locator)**: the rule that an action on a locator matching several elements throws instead of picking one ([30 §4](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#4-locators-page-objects-and-fixtures))
- **page object**: a class that wraps one page's locators and actions so tests never hold selectors ([30 §4](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#4-locators-page-objects-and-fixtures))
- **fixture (Playwright)**: a value `test.extend` builds for a test, with setup before `use` and teardown after; unrelated to Angular's `ComponentFixture` ([30 §4](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#4-locators-page-objects-and-fixtures))
- **worker-scoped fixture**: a fixture declared with `{ scope: 'worker' }`, set up once per worker process and torn down when the worker ends ([30 §4](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#4-locators-page-objects-and-fixtures))
- **test id**: an attribute (`data-testid` by default) that `getByTestId` queries; not something the user perceives ([30 §4](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#4-locators-page-objects-and-fixtures))
- **network-layer mocking**: intercepting the browser's own requests and answering them, so the app runs unchanged against a scripted backend ([30 §5](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#5-api-mocking-at-the-network-layer-and-where-contract-tests-take-over))
- **route handler**: a function registered with `page.route` that receives a `Route` and answers, continues, falls back or aborts a request ([30 §5](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#5-api-mocking-at-the-network-layer-and-where-contract-tests-take-over))
- **stub server**: a small fake backend served over HTTP that tests seed and reset ([30 §5](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#5-api-mocking-at-the-network-layer-and-where-contract-tests-take-over))
- **HAR (HTTP Archive)**: a JSON record of requests and responses that `routeFromHAR` can replay ([30 §5](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#5-api-mocking-at-the-network-layer-and-where-contract-tests-take-over))
- **contract test**: a test that checks both consumer and provider against one shared description of their messages ([30 §5](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#5-api-mocking-at-the-network-layer-and-where-contract-tests-take-over))
- **consumer-driven contract**: a contract the consumer's tests write down (requests made, responses needed) and the provider verifies in its own build ([30 §5](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#5-api-mocking-at-the-network-layer-and-where-contract-tests-take-over))
- **visual regression test**: a test that compares a screenshot with a baseline image and fails when the pixel diff passes a tolerance ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **baseline (visual)**: the approved reference screenshot, stored per project and platform next to the spec ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **pixel diff**: the count (or ratio) of pixels that differ between a screenshot and its baseline ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **threshold** (screenshot): how different two pixels' colours may be before they count as different, from 0 (strict) to 1 (lax); Playwright defaults to 0.2 ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **axe**: a rule engine that checks a rendered page against accessibility rules; `@axe-core/playwright` runs it in end-to-end tests ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **WCAG tag**: an axe label that selects the rules tied to one WCAG level, such as `wcag2aa` ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **ARIA snapshot**: a YAML description of the accessibility tree that a test compares with the page ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **accessibility tree**: the roles, names and states the browser exposes to assistive technology ([30 §6](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#6-visual-regression-and-accessibility-checks-in-end-to-end-tests))
- **flaky test**: a test that gives both a passing and a failing result on the same code ([30 §7](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#7-flaky-tests-root-causes-diagnosis-and-fixes))
- **non-determinism** (testing): an input a test does not control, such as timing, shared data, the clock or the environment, that makes results differ between runs ([30 §7](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#7-flaky-tests-root-causes-diagnosis-and-fixes))
- **quarantine** (testing): moving a flaky test to a separate suite so healthy tests keep giving clear feedback, until it is fixed ([30 §7](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#7-flaky-tests-root-causes-diagnosis-and-fixes))
- **trace** (Playwright): a recording of each action of a test that the Trace Viewer lets you step through ([30 §7](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#7-flaky-tests-root-causes-diagnosis-and-fixes))
- **`--repeat-each`**: a Playwright CLI flag that runs every test N times, to make an intermittent failure appear on demand ([30 §7](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#7-flaky-tests-root-causes-diagnosis-and-fixes))
- **shard**: the slice of a test suite that one CI machine runs, chosen with `--shard=current/total` ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **blob report**: Playwright's raw per-shard results plus attachments, which `merge-reports` joins into one report ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **retries policy**: a team's rule for how many retries CI allows and what a pass on retry triggers ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **flaky budget**: a cap on flaky outcomes past which fixing them comes before new work ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **test data management**: making every test create or reset the data it reads, so tests do not depend on each other ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **tenant** (test data): one isolated slice of the server's data, selected per worker or per test ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **seed** (test data): the known data a reset writes before a test ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **`parallelIndex`**: a Playwright worker's slot from 0 to workers − 1, unique among workers running at the same time ([30 §8](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#8-ci-strategy-for-end-to-end-tests-workers-sharding-retries-policy-and-test-data))
- **test group** (Playwright): the tests one worker runs in order, a whole file by default and a single test with `fullyParallel`; sharding never splits one ([30 §9](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#9-under-the-hood-how-playwrights-runner-groups-shards-retries-and-isolates-tests))
- **dispatcher** (Playwright): the part of the runner that hands test groups to free worker processes and replaces a worker after a failure ([30 §9](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#9-under-the-hood-how-playwrights-runner-groups-shards-retries-and-isolates-tests))
- **outcome** (Playwright): the single verdict for a test after all its attempts: `expected`, `unexpected`, `flaky` or `skipped` ([30 §9](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#9-under-the-hood-how-playwrights-runner-groups-shards-retries-and-isolates-tests))

## Module 31

- **performance loop**: pick a user-visible symptom, measure it, change one thing, and measure again with the same instrument ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **field data**: performance metrics collected from real users' devices and networks ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **lab data**: performance metrics from one controlled run on one machine ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **Chrome UX Report (CrUX)**: Google's public dataset of performance metrics from Chrome users ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **real user monitoring (RUM)**: a script in the page that sends each visit's metrics to your own server ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **Lighthouse**: an automated auditor that runs one controlled page load and reports audits and a score ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **Performance panel**: the Chrome DevTools panel that records what the main thread did during a run ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **main thread**: the browser thread that runs the page's JavaScript, style, layout and paint ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **flame chart**: a recording drawn with time across and the call stack downward ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **Angular DevTools profiler**: the Angular DevTools tab that shows which components' change detection took the time; development builds only ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **performance budget**: a size limit the build enforces so a regression fails before it ships ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers))
- **performance entry**: an object the browser records for one performance event, with a name, a type and a time ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci))
- **PerformanceObserver**: the browser API that subscribes to performance entries of one type ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci))
- **buffered entries**: entries recorded before an observer was created, delivered to it when it observes with `buffered: true` ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci))
- **Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP)**: the command channel Chromium's DevTools use, also open to test tools ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci))
- **CDP session**: one attached connection through which a test sends protocol commands to a page ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci))
- **Lighthouse CI**: a tool that runs Lighthouse in a pipeline, several times per page, and checks the results against assertions ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci))
- **assertion (Lighthouse CI)**: a per-audit check with a level and a limit, such as `["error", {"minScore": 0.8}]` ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci))
- **long animation frame**: a rendering update that takes more than 50 ms ([31 §2](../modules/31-performance.md#2-lab-metrics-in-a-real-browser-performance-observers-devtools-protocol-counters-and-lighthouse-in-ci)).
- **per-check work**: whatever a view's template expressions and hooks compute each time Angular checks that view ([31 §3](../modules/31-performance.md#3-runtime-cost-of-a-change-onpush-zoneless-signals-and-per-check-template-work))
- **derived state**: a value calculated from other signals, in Angular a `computed` ([31 §3](../modules/31-performance.md#3-runtime-cost-of-a-change-onpush-zoneless-signals-and-per-check-template-work))
- **memoization**: returning a stored result while a function's inputs are unchanged ([31 §3](../modules/31-performance.md#3-runtime-cost-of-a-change-onpush-zoneless-signals-and-per-check-template-work))
- **list-wide selector**: one derivation over a whole collection in a store, recomputed for every item when any item changes ([31 §3](../modules/31-performance.md#3-runtime-cost-of-a-change-onpush-zoneless-signals-and-per-check-template-work))
- **virtual scrolling**: keeping only the rows in and near the viewport in the DOM, also called windowing ([31 §4](../modules/31-performance.md#4-long-lists-track-virtual-scrolling-with-the-cdk-and-the-alternatives))
- **rendered range**: the pair of item indexes, start and end, that currently have a row in the DOM ([31 §4](../modules/31-performance.md#4-long-lists-track-virtual-scrolling-with-the-cdk-and-the-alternatives))
- **buffer (virtual scrolling)**: the extra distance rendered beyond the viewport on each side, with a minimum and a maximum ([31 §4](../modules/31-performance.md#4-long-lists-track-virtual-scrolling-with-the-cdk-and-the-alternatives))
- **spacer**: an empty element sized to the whole list so the scrollbar behaves as if every row existed ([31 §4](../modules/31-performance.md#4-long-lists-track-virtual-scrolling-with-the-cdk-and-the-alternatives))
- **row recycling**: reusing a detached row view, kept in a template cache, for the next item that enters the range ([31 §4](../modules/31-performance.md#4-long-lists-track-virtual-scrolling-with-the-cdk-and-the-alternatives))
- **`content-visibility`**: the CSS property that lets the browser skip rendering work for off-screen elements while keeping their nodes ([31 §4](../modules/31-performance.md#4-long-lists-track-virtual-scrolling-with-the-cdk-and-the-alternatives))
- **loading plan**: the decision, for each piece of code, of when it downloads: initial bundle, lazy route, `@defer` block or early fetch ([31 §5](../modules/31-performance.md#5-loading-the-initial-bundle-lazy-routes-defer-and-preloading-as-one-plan))
- **chunk**: one JavaScript file the build emits ([31 §5](../modules/31-performance.md#5-loading-the-initial-bundle-lazy-routes-defer-and-preloading-as-one-plan))
- **initial bundle**: the files the first view needs before it can run ([31 §5](../modules/31-performance.md#5-loading-the-initial-bundle-lazy-routes-defer-and-preloading-as-one-plan))
- **lazy chunk**: a chunk outside the initial bundle, fetched later through a dynamic `import()` ([31 §5](../modules/31-performance.md#5-loading-the-initial-bundle-lazy-routes-defer-and-preloading-as-one-plan))
- **critical path**: the work the browser must finish before the first view appears ([31 §5](../modules/31-performance.md#5-loading-the-initial-bundle-lazy-routes-defer-and-preloading-as-one-plan))
- **budget (build)**: a size limit that warns or fails the build when crossed ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **metafile**: the JSON description of a build, listing each output file's inputs, their bytes and what each input imports ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **`bytesInOutput`**: the bytes one input module contributed to one output file in the metafile ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **importer chain**: the path of imports from an entry to a module, showing who asked for its code ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **tree-shaking**: removing exports nothing uses, only when the bundler can prove removal changes nothing ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **side effect (module)**: a top-level statement that does something when its file loads, such as writing to a global ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **CommonJS**: the older `require`-based module format ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **barrel file**: an `index.ts` that only re-exports other modules ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **source map**: a file mapping positions in minified output back to source files ([31 §6](../modules/31-performance.md#6-bundle-analysis-budgets-and-tree-shaking-pitfalls))
- **preloading (routes)**: fetching lazy route chunks early, on purpose, after the first navigation ([31 §5](../modules/31-performance.md#5-loading-the-initial-bundle-lazy-routes-defer-and-preloading-as-one-plan))
- **prefetching (`@defer`)**: fetching a `@defer` block's chunk on a trigger that fires before the one that renders it ([31 §5](../modules/31-performance.md#5-loading-the-initial-bundle-lazy-routes-defer-and-preloading-as-one-plan))
- **LCP element**: the element the browser reports as the page's largest paint ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **`fetchpriority`**: the HTML attribute hinting how urgently the browser should fetch an image ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **`srcset`**: the HTML attribute listing candidate image files for the browser to choose from ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **`sizes`**: the HTML attribute telling the browser the width the image will render at, so it can choose from a width-descriptor `srcset` ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **density descriptor**: a `srcset` suffix such as `2x`, the file's multiple of the screen's pixel density ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **width descriptor**: a `srcset` suffix such as `640w`, the file's width in pixels ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **image loader**: a function that builds an image URL for a requested width ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **image CDN**: a service that serves resized images at URLs a loader builds ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **placeholder (image)**: a small image or data URL shown until the real image loads ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **`fill` mode**: `NgOptimizedImage`'s option where the image takes its parent's box and carries no `width` or `height` ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **preconnect**: a `<link rel="preconnect">` that opens the connection to another origin early ([31 §7](../modules/31-performance.md#7-images-ngoptimizedimage-with-priority-fill-loaders-and-placeholders))
- **GC root**: a starting point the garbage collector always keeps, such as `window`; anything reachable from it stays in memory ([31 §8](../modules/31-performance.md#8-memory-leaks-heap-snapshots-detached-nodes-and-the-devtools-workflow))
- **retainer**: an object on the path from a GC root that keeps another object alive by pointing at it ([31 §8](../modules/31-performance.md#8-memory-leaks-heap-snapshots-detached-nodes-and-the-devtools-workflow))
- **retainer path**: the chain of retainers from a GC root to a retained object ([31 §8](../modules/31-performance.md#8-memory-leaks-heap-snapshots-detached-nodes-and-the-devtools-workflow))
- **detached node**: a DOM element removed from the document but still referenced, so it is not collected ([31 §8](../modules/31-performance.md#8-memory-leaks-heap-snapshots-detached-nodes-and-the-devtools-workflow))
- **heap snapshot**: a DevTools record of every object in the JavaScript heap at one moment ([31 §8](../modules/31-performance.md#8-memory-leaks-heap-snapshots-detached-nodes-and-the-devtools-workflow))
- **allocation timeline**: a DevTools recording of where objects were allocated over a period instead of at one moment ([31 §8](../modules/31-performance.md#8-memory-leaks-heap-snapshots-detached-nodes-and-the-devtools-workflow))
- **`Runtime.queryObjects`**: the CDP method that returns the live objects with a given prototype ([31 §8](../modules/31-performance.md#8-memory-leaks-heap-snapshots-detached-nodes-and-the-devtools-workflow))
- **Web Worker**: a separate thread with its own global scope and no DOM, which talks to the page only by messages ([31 §9](../modules/31-performance.md#9-off-the-main-thread-web-workers-in-angular-and-runoutsideangular-in-zone-apps))
- **message passing**: sending data between page and worker with `postMessage`, received as `message` events ([31 §9](../modules/31-performance.md#9-off-the-main-thread-web-workers-in-angular-and-runoutsideangular-in-zone-apps))
- **structured clone**: the algorithm that copies a message's data between contexts; functions and DOM nodes make it throw `DataCloneError` ([31 §9](../modules/31-performance.md#9-off-the-main-thread-web-workers-in-angular-and-runoutsideangular-in-zone-apps))
- **transferable**: an object that owns a resource that can be moved to another context, so it is available in only one context at a time ([31 §9](../modules/31-performance.md#9-off-the-main-thread-web-workers-in-angular-and-runoutsideangular-in-zone-apps))
- **`import.meta.url`**: the URL of the current module, which the builder reads in `new Worker(new URL('./x.worker', import.meta.url))` ([31 §9](../modules/31-performance.md#9-off-the-main-thread-web-workers-in-angular-and-runoutsideangular-in-zone-apps))
- **CSR (client-side rendering)**: the server sends a nearly empty shell and the browser builds the page by running JavaScript ([31 §10](../modules/31-performance.md#10-rendering-strategy-as-a-performance-decision-csr-ssr-and-ssg))
- **SSR (server-side rendering)**: the server renders the HTML for each request ([31 §10](../modules/31-performance.md#10-rendering-strategy-as-a-performance-decision-csr-ssr-and-ssg))
- **SSG (static site generation, prerendering)**: the HTML is rendered once at build time and served as a file ([31 §10](../modules/31-performance.md#10-rendering-strategy-as-a-performance-decision-csr-ssr-and-ssg))
- **hydration (as used in 31)**: making server-rendered HTML interactive on the client by reusing its DOM; owned by module 32 ([31 §10](../modules/31-performance.md#10-rendering-strategy-as-a-performance-decision-csr-ssr-and-ssg))
- **breakpoint (image)**: a width in pixels, listed in `IMAGE_CONFIG`, for which the loader builds one `srcset` file ([31 §11](../modules/31-performance.md#11-under-the-hood-the-cdks-rendered-range-ngoptimizedimages-srcset-and-the-builders-chunks))
- **chunk optimization**: the Angular builder's second bundling pass over esbuild's output, which can merge lazy chunks and rewrites the metafile ([31 §11](../modules/31-performance.md#11-under-the-hood-the-cdks-rendered-range-ngoptimizedimages-srcset-and-the-builders-chunks))

## Module 32

- **render mode**: the per-route choice of where and when the HTML is built, a `RenderMode` value (`Client`, `Server`, `Prerender`) in the server routes ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **CSR (client-side rendering)**: the server sends an empty app root and the browser builds the page by running JavaScript ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **SSR (server-side rendering)**: the server runs Angular for each request and sends finished HTML ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **SSG (static site generation, prerendering)**: Angular renders the page once at build time and the server sends that file ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **hybrid rendering**: mixing CSR, SSR and SSG in one app, route by route ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **hydration**: making server-rendered HTML interactive by reusing its DOM instead of rebuilding it ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **crawler**: a program that fetches pages to index them ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **link preview (unfurl)**: the card a chat or social app builds from a pasted URL ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **Open Graph tags**: `<meta property="og:…">` elements in the `<head>` defined by the Open Graph protocol ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **time to first byte (TTFB)**: the time between starting to navigate to a page and the first byte of its response arriving ([32 §1](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#1-why-render-on-the-server-csr-ssr-ssg-and-hybrid-rendering-and-what-each-costs))
- **server entry point**: the file the server bundle's application starts from, `main.server.ts` in a generated project ([32 §2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#2-adding-angularssr-what-ng-add-generates-the-build-output-and-the-server-entry))
- **BootstrapContext**: the object `bootstrapApplication` takes on the server to carry a platform created for the request ([32 §2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#2-adding-angularssr-what-ng-add-generates-the-build-output-and-the-server-entry))
- **server config**: `app.config.server.ts`, which merges server-only providers into the shared application config ([32 §2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#2-adding-angularssr-what-ng-add-generates-the-build-output-and-the-server-entry))
- **mergeApplicationConfig**: the core function that merges application configurations from left to right ([32 §2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#2-adding-angularssr-what-ng-add-generates-the-build-output-and-the-server-entry))
- **Express middleware**: a function `(req, res, next)` that answers a request or calls `next()` to pass it on ([32 §2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#2-adding-angularssr-what-ng-add-generates-the-build-output-and-the-server-entry))
- **request handler**: the exported function that wraps the Express app for callers such as the Angular CLI's dev server and build ([32 §2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#2-adding-angularssr-what-ng-add-generates-the-build-output-and-the-server-entry))
- **outputMode**: the application builder option choosing a server build or a static one (`'server'` or `'static'`) ([32 §2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#2-adding-angularssr-what-ng-add-generates-the-build-output-and-the-server-entry))
- **server route**: an entry of the `ServerRoute[]` given to `withRoutes` that names a router path and the `renderMode` that path renders with ([32 §3](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#3-server-routes-and-rendermode-per-route-rendering-getprerenderparams-fallbacks-headers-and-status))
- **getPrerenderParams**: the function on a parameterized `Prerender` server route that returns the parameter values to build at build time ([32 §3](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#3-server-routes-and-rendermode-per-route-rendering-getprerenderparams-fallbacks-headers-and-status))
- **prerender fallback**: what a parameterized `Prerender` route does for a value it did not build: `Server` (default), `Client` or `None` ([32 §3](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#3-server-routes-and-rendermode-per-route-rendering-getprerenderparams-fallbacks-headers-and-status))
- **soft redirect**: a redirect written as an HTML page with `<meta http-equiv="refresh">`, used where there is no response to carry a `Location` header ([32 §3](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#3-server-routes-and-rendermode-per-route-rendering-getprerenderparams-fallbacks-headers-and-status))
- **route header**: a `headers` entry of a server route, sent with that route's response ([32 §3](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#3-server-routes-and-rendermode-per-route-rendering-getprerenderparams-fallbacks-headers-and-status))
- **destructive hydration**: discarding the server's DOM and rendering the page again in the browser, Angular's behavior before non-destructive hydration (16.0) ([32 §4](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#4-hydration-reusing-the-servers-dom-mismatches-and-ngskiphydration))
- **claim (a node)**: during hydration, keeping an existing server node as if Angular had created it, found by position ([32 §4](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#4-hydration-reusing-the-servers-dom-mismatches-and-ngskiphydration))
- **hydration annotation (`ngh`)**: the `ngh` attribute on a component host pointing at an `__nghData__` entry in the state script that records what the view rendered ([32 §4](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#4-hydration-reusing-the-servers-dom-mismatches-and-ngskiphydration))
- **hydration mismatch**: a DOM that differs from what the template expects at hydration, raised as an NG05xx error ([32 §4](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#4-hydration-reusing-the-servers-dom-mismatches-and-ngskiphydration))
- **ngSkipHydration**: an attribute on a component host that opts the component out of hydration: the browser clears and re-renders it ([32 §4](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#4-hydration-reusing-the-servers-dom-mismatches-and-ngskiphydration))
- **integrity marker**: the `<!--nghm-->` comment the server puts first in `<body>`; the client refuses to hydrate if it is gone ([32 §4](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#4-hydration-reusing-the-servers-dom-mismatches-and-ngskiphydration))
- **event replay**: recording user events on server-rendered HTML before hydration and delivering them to the real listeners afterwards ([32 §5](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#5-event-replay-keeping-the-clicks-made-before-the-code-arrives))
- **event contract**: the small inline script that listens from the start of `<body>` and queues the events for replay ([32 §5](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#5-event-replay-keeping-the-clicks-made-before-the-code-arrives))
- **jsaction (attribute)**: the attribute the server writes on an element with a listener, naming the event types to capture, such as `jsaction="click:;"` ([32 §5](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#5-event-replay-keeping-the-clicks-made-before-the-code-arrives))
- **JSAction (library)**: the event-delegation library behind the `jsaction` name that Angular's event contract comes from ([32 §5](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#5-event-replay-keeping-the-clicks-made-before-the-code-arrives))
- **capture versus replay**: capture records an event before hydration; replay delivers it to the real listener after hydration ([32 §5](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#5-event-replay-keeping-the-clicks-made-before-the-code-arrives))
- **incremental hydration**: hydrating a server-rendered page block by block, each `@defer` block with a hydrate trigger only when its trigger fires ([32 §6](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#6-incremental-hydration-defer-blocks-with-hydrate-triggers))
- **hydrate trigger**: a `hydrate on …`, `hydrate when` or `hydrate never` condition on a `@defer` block that makes the server render its content and decides when the browser hydrates it ([32 §6](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#6-incremental-hydration-defer-blocks-with-hydrate-triggers))
- **dehydrated block**: a server-rendered `@defer` block whose code has not loaded in the browser: server DOM, no listeners ([32 §6](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#6-incremental-hydration-defer-blocks-with-hydrate-triggers))
- **hydration boundary**: the edge of a `@defer` block with hydrate triggers, where hydration of the rest of the page stops ([32 §6](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#6-incremental-hydration-defer-blocks-with-hydrate-triggers))
- **platform ID**: the value of the `PLATFORM_ID` token, `'browser'` or `'server'`, that tells code which environment it runs in ([32 §7](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#7-platform-safe-code-browser-apis-afternextrender-document-request-and-response_init))
- **server platform**: the platform Angular creates to render on Node: it provides the `'server'` platform ID, a DOM emulation and the server-side services ([32 §7](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#7-platform-safe-code-browser-apis-afternextrender-document-request-and-response_init))
- **DOM emulation**: the server's `document` and elements, built by the domino library in `@angular/platform-server`, with no layout or styles ([32 §7](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#7-platform-safe-code-browser-apis-afternextrender-document-request-and-response_init))
- **platform-specific provider**: a provider that supplies a different class or value in the browser and on the server, by a factory on `PLATFORM_ID` or by a provider in `app.config.server.ts` ([32 §7](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#7-platform-safe-code-browser-apis-afternextrender-document-request-and-response_init))
- **REQUEST**: the `@angular/core` token holding the Fetch `Request` of the page being rendered on the server, `null` in the browser and in prerendered pages ([32 §7](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#7-platform-safe-code-browser-apis-afternextrender-document-request-and-response_init))
- **RESPONSE_INIT**: the `@angular/core` token holding the mutable `ResponseInit` (status, headers) of the page being rendered on the server, `null` in the browser and in prerendered pages ([32 §7](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#7-platform-safe-code-browser-apis-afternextrender-document-request-and-response_init))
- **REQUEST_CONTEXT**: the `@angular/core` token holding the `unknown` value the server passes as the second argument of `handle` ([32 §7](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#7-platform-safe-code-browser-apis-afternextrender-document-request-and-response_init))
- **state transfer**: handing a value from the server render to the browser by writing it into the page as JSON, which the browser reads during hydration ([32 §8](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#8-data-across-the-boundary-the-http-transfer-cache-transferstate-and-resource-ids))
- **state script**: the `<script id="ng-state" type="application/json">` element in the server HTML that holds the transferred JSON ([32 §8](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#8-data-across-the-boundary-the-http-transfer-cache-transferstate-and-resource-ids))
- **HTTP transfer cache**: the `HttpClient` feature that stores the `GET` and `HEAD` responses of the server render in the state script and answers the same requests in the browser until the app is stable ([32 §8](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#8-data-across-the-boundary-the-http-transfer-cache-transferstate-and-resource-ids))
- **state key**: the string a value is stored under in `TransferState` and the state script; `makeStateKey` returns the string it is given ([32 §8](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#8-data-across-the-boundary-the-http-transfer-cache-transferstate-and-resource-ids))
- **origin map**: the server-only `HTTP_TRANSFER_CACHE_ORIGIN_MAP` token that renames the origin the server calls to the origin the browser calls, so the transfer cache sees the same request ([32 §8](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#8-data-across-the-boundary-the-http-transfer-cache-transferstate-and-resource-ids))
- **AngularAppEngine**: the `@angular/ssr` engine that turns a web-standard `Request` into a `Response` (or `null`), so it runs on any runtime with the Fetch types; `AngularNodeAppEngine` wraps it for Node ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts))
- **edge runtime**: a JavaScript runtime at a CDN's locations, without Node's modules; Angular targets it with `ssr.platform: 'neutral'` ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts))
- **static output**: the build from `outputMode: 'static'`, plain files with no server entry ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts))
- **shared cache**: a cache between the server and many users (a CDN or reverse proxy) that can give one stored response to everyone ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts))
- **Cache-Control**: the HTTP header telling caches whether and how long to store a response ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts); directives in [09 §4](../modules/09-web-networking-storage-security.md#4-http-caching))
- **host validation**: the engine's check of `Host` (and trusted proxy host headers) against `allowedHosts`, answering 400 for an unknown host since v22 ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts))
- **SSRF (server-side request forgery)**: an attack that makes a server send requests to an address the attacker chooses ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts), [33](../modules/33-security.md))
- **proxy headers**: `Forwarded` and `X-Forwarded-*`, which a load balancer adds to pass on the client's original host and scheme; ignored unless `trustProxyHeaders` keeps them ([32 §9](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#9-deploying-ssr-node-and-other-runtimes-static-output-caching-rendered-pages-and-allowed-hosts))
- **server app**: the internal `@angular/ssr` object (`AngularServerApp`) that holds one build's server routes, HTML templates and bootstrap function and answers a request by serving, sending the shell or rendering ([32 §10](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#10-under-the-hood-a-request-through-the-engine-the-serialized-page-and-the-clients-hydration-pass))
- **route tree**: the server routes stored segment by segment (`:id` as `*`), which the server app matches a URL against to find its render mode ([32 §10](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#10-under-the-hood-a-request-through-the-engine-the-serialized-page-and-the-clients-hydration-pass))
- **serialization (SSR)**: the last server step, writing into the HTML what hydration needs: the integrity marker, `ngh` annotations, the event replay call, `ng-server-context` and the state script ([32 §10](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#10-under-the-hood-a-request-through-the-engine-the-serialized-page-and-the-clients-hydration-pass))
- **server context attribute**: `ng-server-context` on the root element, `ssg` for a prerendered page and `ssr` for a server-rendered one; a client-rendered shell has none ([32 §10](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#10-under-the-hood-a-request-through-the-engine-the-serialized-page-and-the-clients-hydration-pass))
- **fallback text**: the text the server HTML shows where a browser-only value will go, replaced after hydration by `afterNextRender` ([32 Exercise 32.2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#ex32-2))
- **no-op store**: a storage implementation whose reads return nothing and whose writes do nothing, provided on the server so the same component runs there ([32 Exercise 32.2](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#ex32-2))
- **server stamp**: a value the server makes once per render and the browser reuses instead of making its own, carried by `TransferState` or a `resource` with an `id` ([32 Exercise 32.3](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#ex32-3))
- **dehydrated block registry**: the internal map (`DEHYDRATED_BLOCK_REGISTRY`) from a server-rendered `@defer` block's id to its view, where the browser keeps blocks that still wait for a hydrate trigger ([32 Q32.27](../modules/32-ssr-ssg-hydration.md#q32-27))

## Module 33

- **threat model**: a list of what you protect, who can attack it, from where, and which control stops each attack ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **asset**: what a threat model protects: data, money, a session ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **attack surface**: every input an attacker can reach ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **trust boundary**: where data passes from a less trusted side to a more trusted one; in a single-page app, the line between the browser and the API ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **OWASP Top 10**: OWASP's list of web-application risk categories; the guide uses the 2021 edition ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **SSRF (server-side request forgery)**: making a server fetch a URL the attacker chose ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **supply chain**: the packages and tools a build is made of, and the risk that one of them is malicious ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **defense in depth**: layering independent controls so that one failure is not a breach ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **stored XSS**: cross-site scripting where the server saves the payload and serves it to other users ([33 §2](../modules/33-security.md#2-xss-and-angulars-template-model-escaping-by-default-trusted-templates-and-the-paths-around-them))
- **reflected XSS**: cross-site scripting where a response echoes part of the request that carried the payload ([33 §2](../modules/33-security.md#2-xss-and-angulars-template-model-escaping-by-default-trusted-templates-and-the-paths-around-them))
- **DOM-based XSS**: cross-site scripting where the page's own script moves attacker data into the DOM and the response does not change ([33 §2](../modules/33-security.md#2-xss-and-angulars-template-model-escaping-by-default-trusted-templates-and-the-paths-around-them))
- **payload**: the attacker's input, such as markup or script text, that an attack tries to get a page to run ([33 §2](../modules/33-security.md#2-xss-and-angulars-template-model-escaping-by-default-trusted-templates-and-the-paths-around-them))
- **template injection**: attacker text becoming part of a template, so the template compiler runs it as code ([33 §2](../modules/33-security.md#2-xss-and-angulars-template-model-escaping-by-default-trusted-templates-and-the-paths-around-them))
- **security context**: the kind of place a bound value lands (HTML, style, URL, resource URL), which decides the rule Angular applies ([33 §3](../modules/33-security.md#3-sanitization-by-security-context-html-style-url-and-resource-url))
- **sanitization**: cleaning a value so it is safe for one security context while keeping it usable; escaping, by contrast, turns it into text ([33 §3](../modules/33-security.md#3-sanitization-by-security-context-html-style-url-and-resource-url))
- **allowlist**: a list of what is permitted, with everything else dropped ([33 §3](../modules/33-security.md#3-sanitization-by-security-context-html-style-url-and-resource-url))
- **`unsafe:` prefix**: what Angular prepends to a URL it rejects ([33 §3](../modules/33-security.md#3-sanitization-by-security-context-html-style-url-and-resource-url))
- **resource URL**: a URL the browser loads and runs as code, such as an iframe `src` ([33 §3](../modules/33-security.md#3-sanitization-by-security-context-html-style-url-and-resource-url))
- **mutation XSS**: markup that changes when it is parsed again, so a checked string turns dangerous ([33 §3](../modules/33-security.md#3-sanitization-by-security-context-html-style-url-and-resource-url))
- **bypass**: a `DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrust*` call, which wraps a string in a safe value and so turns Angular's check off for it ([33 §4](../modules/33-security.md#4-domsanitizer-and-bypasssecuritytrust-when-trusting-a-value-is-dangerous-and-the-safe-patterns))
- **safe value**: the wrapper object (`SafeHtml`, `SafeStyle`, `SafeScript`, `SafeUrl`, `SafeResourceUrl`) a bypass returns; a binding accepts it only in its own context ([33 §4](../modules/33-security.md#4-domsanitizer-and-bypasssecuritytrust-when-trusting-a-value-is-dangerous-and-the-safe-patterns))
- **canonical URL**: a URL you build yourself from parsed, checked parts instead of passing the input through ([33 §4](../modules/33-security.md#4-domsanitizer-and-bypasssecuritytrust-when-trusting-a-value-is-dangerous-and-the-safe-patterns))
- **embed allowlist**: the hosts and ID shapes an app accepts for embedded content ([33 §4](../modules/33-security.md#4-domsanitizer-and-bypasssecuritytrust-when-trusting-a-value-is-dangerous-and-the-safe-patterns))
- **strict CSP**: a Content Security Policy that trusts scripts by nonce or hash plus `'strict-dynamic'`, never by host ([33 §5](../modules/33-security.md#5-content-security-policy-with-angular-nonces-csp_nonce-and-autocsp))
- **`ngCspNonce`**: the root-element attribute that carries the response's nonce to Angular and to the build's nonce copying ([33 §5](../modules/33-security.md#5-content-security-policy-with-angular-nonces-csp_nonce-and-autocsp))
- **`CSP_NONCE`**: the `@angular/core` injection token for the nonce Angular puts on the `<style>` elements it adds; by default it reads `ngCspNonce` ([33 §5](../modules/33-security.md#5-content-security-policy-with-angular-nonces-csp_nonce-and-autocsp))
- **`autoCsp`**: the build option (`security.autoCsp`) that writes a hash-based script policy into `index.html` as a `<meta>` tag; client-rendered builds only ([33 §5](../modules/33-security.md#5-content-security-policy-with-angular-nonces-csp_nonce-and-autocsp))
- **hash-based policy**: a CSP that trusts inline scripts by the SHA-256 of their text, so a cached page stays valid ([33 §5](../modules/33-security.md#5-content-security-policy-with-angular-nonces-csp_nonce-and-autocsp))
- **violation report**: the JSON body a browser posts to a policy's reporting endpoint when something breaks the policy ([33 §5](../modules/33-security.md#5-content-security-policy-with-angular-nonces-csp_nonce-and-autocsp))
- **report-only mode**: a policy sent as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`, which reports violations without blocking anything ([33 §5](../modules/33-security.md#5-content-security-policy-with-angular-nonces-csp_nonce-and-autocsp))
- **Trusted Types policy**: a named object from `trustedTypes.createPolicy(name, rules)` whose rules turn a string into a typed value a sink accepts; Angular's are `angular` and `angular#unsafe-bypass` ([33 §6](../modules/33-security.md#6-trusted-types-with-angular-the-frameworks-policies-enforcement-and-rollout))
- **`TrustedHTML`**: the typed value an HTML sink such as `innerHTML` accepts when Trusted Types are enforced ([33 §6](../modules/33-security.md#6-trusted-types-with-angular-the-frameworks-policies-enforcement-and-rollout))
