- **updateMode**: an asset group's setting for changed files when a new version arrives: `prefetch` at once, `lazy` on next request ([37 §5](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#5-caching-with-asset-and-data-groups-navigation-requests-and-the-app-shell))
- **navigation request**: a `GET` with mode `navigate` that accepts HTML and matches `navigationUrls`, answered with the version's `index.html` ([37 §5](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#5-caching-with-asset-and-data-groups-navigation-requests-and-the-app-shell))
- **ngsw-bypass**: a request header or query parameter that makes the Angular worker leave the request to the browser ([37 §5](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#5-caching-with-asset-and-data-groups-navigation-requests-and-the-app-shell))
- **app shell**: a route rendered at build time so a static page appears at once; documented only here ([37 §5](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#5-caching-with-asset-and-data-groups-navigation-requests-and-the-app-shell))

- **version hash (service worker)**: the SHA-1 of an `ngsw.json`, which names one app version inside the worker ([37 §6](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#6-updates-versions-swupdate-prompting-a-reload-unrecoverable-states-and-the-kill-switch))
- **VERSION_READY**: the `SwUpdate.versionUpdates` event saying a newer version is cached while the tab still runs an older one ([37 §6](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#6-updates-versions-swupdate-prompting-a-reload-unrecoverable-states-and-the-kill-switch))
- **unrecoverable state**: a tab whose app version the worker can no longer serve, so only a reload helps ([37 §6](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#6-updates-versions-swupdate-prompting-a-reload-unrecoverable-states-and-the-kill-switch))
- **SAFE_MODE**: the Angular worker's state after its own initialization fails, serving traffic from the network ([37 §6](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#6-updates-versions-swupdate-prompting-a-reload-unrecoverable-states-and-the-kill-switch))
- **safety worker**: `safety-worker.js`, a worker that unregisters itself and deletes the Angular worker's caches, served as a kill switch ([37 §6](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#6-updates-versions-swupdate-prompting-a-reload-unrecoverable-states-and-the-kill-switch))

- **push service**: the browser vendor's server that delivers push messages to a browser ([37 §7](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#7-push-notifications-and-installability-swpush-notification-clicks-and-the-manifest))
- **subscription (push)**: the push service's record for one worker, an endpoint URL plus keys, which your backend stores and sends to ([37 §7](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#7-push-notifications-and-installability-swpush-notification-clicks-and-the-manifest))
- **VAPID key**: the P-256 public key passed when subscribing, which the push service uses to authenticate your application server ([37 §7](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#7-push-notifications-and-installability-swpush-notification-clicks-and-the-manifest))
- **notification payload**: the JSON a backend sends as a push message, whose `notification` field the Angular worker turns into a shown notification ([37 §7](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#7-push-notifications-and-installability-swpush-notification-clicks-and-the-manifest))
- **web app manifest**: the JSON file describing an installable site (named in [09 §9](../modules/09-web-networking-storage-security.md#9-service-workers-and-the-pwa-manifest), tested for the lab in [37 §7](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#7-push-notifications-and-installability-swpush-notification-clicks-and-the-manifest))
- **ErrorHandler**: the class with a `handleError(error)` method that Angular sends every error it catches to; you replace it by providing your own under the `ErrorHandler` token ([37 §8](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#8-errorhandler-and-global-listeners-what-reaches-the-handler-and-what-does-not))
- **environment initializer**: a function run when an environment injector is created (defined in [16 §7](../modules/16-dependency-injection.md#7-injectiontoken-multi-providers-environment-providers-and-initializers), used in [37 §8](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#8-errorhandler-and-global-listeners-what-reaches-the-handler-and-what-does-not))
- **window error event**: the browser's report of an uncaught exception, which `provideBrowserGlobalErrorListeners()` forwards to the `ErrorHandler` ([37 §8](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#8-errorhandler-and-global-listeners-what-reaches-the-handler-and-what-does-not))
- **unhandled rejection**: a promise that rejects with no handler (defined in [04 §8](../modules/04-js-async-event-loop.md#8-unhandled-rejections-queuemicrotask-and-starvation), forwarded in [37 §8](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#8-errorhandler-and-global-listeners-what-reaches-the-handler-and-what-does-not))
- **error boundary**: a template region, written with `@boundary`, whose content is replaced by a fallback when it throws while created or checked ([37 §9](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#9-error-boundaries-boundary-error-reset-onviewerror-and-onerror))
- **fallback (error boundary)**: the `@error` block a boundary renders in place of content that threw ([37 §9](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#9-error-boundaries-boundary-error-reset-onviewerror-and-onerror))
- **`$error`**: the implicit variable holding the caught error inside an `@error` block ([37 §9](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#9-error-boundaries-boundary-error-reset-onviewerror-and-onerror))
- **`$reset`**: the implicit function in an `@error` block that clears the boundary's error and renders its content again ([37 §9](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#9-error-boundaries-boundary-error-reset-onviewerror-and-onerror))
- **`ErrorDetails`**: the second argument of `onViewError` and `onError`: the component whose view threw and, for a boundary, its host component and `reset` ([37 §9](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#9-error-boundaries-boundary-error-reset-onviewerror-and-onerror))
- **developer preview**: an Angular API label for APIs that "can change at any time, even in new patch versions" ([12 §1](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#1-what-a-framework-does-and-angulars-philosophy), [37 §9](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#9-error-boundaries-boundary-error-reset-onviewerror-and-onerror))
- **expected error**: a failure the screen that made the call can name and recover from, such as a 404 for an optional resource ([37 §10](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#10-error-ux-expected-and-unexpected-errors-http-failures-offline-state-and-route-errors))
- **unexpected error**: a failure nobody on the screen can fix, sent to the `ErrorHandler` with a generic message ([37 §10](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#10-error-ux-expected-and-unexpected-errors-http-failures-offline-state-and-route-errors))
- **error mapping layer**: the one place that turns an `HttpErrorResponse` into errors the UI can render (defined in [27 §5](../modules/27-http-client.md#5-errors-httperrorresponse-an-error-mapping-layer-and-retry-with-backoff-and-jitter), used in [37 §10](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#10-error-ux-expected-and-unexpected-errors-http-failures-offline-state-and-route-errors))
- **offline state**: the browser's report that it has no network, shown as a banner while the app keeps working ([37 §10](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#10-error-ux-expected-and-unexpected-errors-http-failures-offline-state-and-route-errors))
- **reporter port**: an injection token with one method, `send`, that the `ErrorHandler` calls so no vendor code sits in the handler ([37 §11](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#11-logging-and-error-tracking-a-reporter-port-context-privacy-source-maps-and-sampling), token in [16 §7](../modules/16-dependency-injection.md#7-injectiontoken-multi-providers-environment-providers-and-initializers))
- **adapter (reporter)**: the code behind the reporter port that sends a report to a vendor SDK or to your own backend ([37 §11](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#11-logging-and-error-tracking-a-reporter-port-context-privacy-source-maps-and-sampling))
- **sampling**: keeping only a fraction of reports to limit volume ([37 §11](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#11-logging-and-error-tracking-a-reporter-port-context-privacy-source-maps-and-sampling))
- **de-duplication (reports)**: sending the same error once inside a time window ([37 §11](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#11-logging-and-error-tracking-a-reporter-port-context-privacy-source-maps-and-sampling))
- **hidden source map**: a map the build writes for error reporting tools, set with `sourceMap.hidden` ([37 §11](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#11-logging-and-error-tracking-a-reporter-port-context-privacy-source-maps-and-sampling))
- **PII**: personally identifiable information, anything that points at one person, such as an e-mail address ([37 §11](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#11-logging-and-error-tracking-a-reporter-port-context-privacy-source-maps-and-sampling))
- **peer dependency (library evaluation)**: a package a library expects the app to install, within its `peerDependencies` range; the first rule of the evaluation table ([37 §12](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#12-the-production-ecosystem-and-how-to-evaluate-a-library))
- **`sideEffects` (library evaluation)**: a `package.json` hint that a package's files are "pure" and "safe to prune if unused" (webpack); `@angular/elements` and `@angular/service-worker` set it to `false` ([37 §12](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#12-the-production-ecosystem-and-how-to-evaluate-a-library))
- **license allow-list**: the licenses your legal team approved; a rule of the evaluation table ([37 §12](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#12-the-production-ecosystem-and-how-to-evaluate-a-library))
- **spike (library evaluation)**: a throwaway branch that adds a candidate library to your real build to measure it ([37 §12](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#12-the-production-ecosystem-and-how-to-evaluate-a-library))

- **strategy (Angular Elements)**: the object behind an Angular element that creates, updates and destroys its component through `connect`, `disconnect`, `setInputValue`, `getInputValue` and `events` ([37 §13](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#13-under-the-hood-createcustomelements-strategy-and-the-service-workers-driver))
- **strategy factory (Angular Elements)**: the object that builds one strategy per element; replaceable through `createCustomElement`'s `strategyFactory` option ([37 §13](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#13-under-the-hood-createcustomelements-strategy-and-the-service-workers-driver))
- **driver (Angular service worker)**: the internal main class of `ngsw-worker.js` that assigns versions, checks for updates and holds the worker's state ([37 §13](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#13-under-the-hood-createcustomelements-strategy-and-the-service-workers-driver))
- **version map (Angular service worker)**: the driver's internal map from each tab's client ID to the version hash serving it ([37 §13](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#13-under-the-hood-createcustomelements-strategy-and-the-service-workers-driver))

- **roving tabindex**: a group of controls with exactly one tab stop that moves with the arrow keys ([37 Exercise 37.2](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#ex37-2))

- **de-duplication window**: the time during which a repeat of the same error from the same widget counts as the one already reported ([37 Exercise 37.3](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#ex37-3))

- **update banner**: a notice that a new app version is ready, with a button that reloads the page into it ([37 Exercise 37.4](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#ex37-4))

- **schema (Angular template)**: a setting that tells the template compiler which unknown element or property names to accept instead of reporting an error ([37 Q37.05](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#q37-05))

- **ngsw-config.json**: the file you write with the Angular worker's rules (asset groups, data groups, navigation URLs, `appData`); the build reads it and the worker never does ([37 Q37.09](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#q37-09))
- **ngsw-worker.js**: the prebuilt worker script the build copies from `@angular/service-worker`; it reads `ngsw.json`, installs versions and serves requests from them ([37 Q37.09](../modules/37-elements-pwa-errors-ecosystem.md#q37-09))

## Module 38

- **feature area**: a part of the app named for what the user does, such as orders or catalog, that owns its own folder ([38 §1](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#1-folder-structures-that-scale-by-feature-domain-slices-and-core-shared-and-feature-boundaries))
- **domain slice**: a larger feature area kept as a folder with its own sub-features ([38 §1](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#1-folder-structures-that-scale-by-feature-domain-slices-and-core-shared-and-feature-boundaries))
- **core (folder)**: app-wide singletons and setup, such as providers, interceptors and the layout shell, configured once ([38 §1](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#1-folder-structures-that-scale-by-feature-domain-slices-and-core-shared-and-feature-boundaries))
- **shared (folder)**: code with no feature knowledge, such as ui primitives, pipes and utilities ([38 §1](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#1-folder-structures-that-scale-by-feature-domain-slices-and-core-shared-and-feature-boundaries))
- **boundary (folder)**: the line a folder draws so that outside files use only what it exposes ([38 §1](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#1-folder-structures-that-scale-by-feature-domain-slices-and-core-shared-and-feature-boundaries))
- **barrel file**: an `index.ts` that re-exports a folder's public names ([38 §1](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#1-folder-structures-that-scale-by-feature-domain-slices-and-core-shared-and-feature-boundaries))
- **presentational component**: a component that gets data through inputs, sends events through outputs and injects no data services ([38 §2](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#2-smart-container-and-presentational-components))
- **container component**: a component that injects the facade, passes its signals down and turns outputs into facade calls ([38 §2](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#2-smart-container-and-presentational-components))
- **signal input**: an input declared with `input()` that the component reads as a signal ([38 §2](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#2-smart-container-and-presentational-components))
- **output**: an event a component emits with `output()` for its parent to handle ([38 §2](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#2-smart-container-and-presentational-components))
- **OnPush**: the change-detection strategy that re-checks a component when an input changes, an event fires or a signal it reads changes ([38 §2](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#2-smart-container-and-presentational-components))
- **data-access layer**: the part of a feature that knows where data comes from: HTTP services, the store and DTO mapping ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **DTO**: data transfer object, the shape the server sends ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **view model**: the shape the screens use, mapped from a DTO ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **facade (feature)**: one class in front of a feature's store and API that exposes read-only signals and intent methods ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **intent method**: a facade method named for what the user wants, such as `load()` or `select(id)` ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **port**: a token that names what a class needs from an outside source, such as `ORDERS_API` ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **adapter (data access)**: a class that gives one data source the shape a port promises, such as `HttpOrdersApi` ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **service-with-signals store**: a service with private state in a signal, `computed` selectors and methods as the only writers ([38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer))
- **library type**: what a project's code does: `feature`, `ui`, `data-access` or `util` ([38 §4](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#4-layers-library-types-and-dependency-rules))
- **scope**: who owns a project's code, such as `orders`, `catalog` or `shared` ([38 §4](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#4-layers-library-types-and-dependency-rules))
- **dependency rule**: a one-way direction between library types and scopes that imports must follow ([38 §4](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#4-layers-library-types-and-dependency-rules))
- **tag (Nx)**: a label on a project, such as `type:ui`, that boundary rules match ([38 §4](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#4-layers-library-types-and-dependency-rules))
- **relative import**: an import that names a file by its position, such as `../../orders/x` ([38 §4](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#4-layers-library-types-and-dependency-rules))
- **ng-packagr**: the build tool behind `ng build ui-lib` that writes a library in the Angular Package Format ([38 §5](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#5-angular-cli-libraries-ng-generate-library-ng-packagr-and-consuming-the-build))
- **Angular Package Format**: the folder layout of a built library: `package.json` with `exports`, FESM bundles and bundled typings ([38 §5](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#5-angular-cli-libraries-ng-generate-library-ng-packagr-and-consuming-the-build))
- **FESM**: a flat ES module, one file holding all of an entry point's code ([38 §5](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#5-angular-cli-libraries-ng-generate-library-ng-packagr-and-consuming-the-build))
- **entry point**: a folder of a library with its own public surface and bundle ([38 §5](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#5-angular-cli-libraries-ng-generate-library-ng-packagr-and-consuming-the-build))
- **paths mapping**: a `tsconfig` entry that maps an import name to a location, such as `ui-lib` to `./dist/ui-lib` ([38 §5](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#5-angular-cli-libraries-ng-generate-library-ng-packagr-and-consuming-the-build))
- **peer dependency**: a package range the consumer's app must install itself, such as `@angular/core` ([38 §5](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#5-angular-cli-libraries-ng-generate-library-ng-packagr-and-consuming-the-build))
- **public API surface**: everything a consumer can import and rely on; for a library, what its entry files export ([38 §6](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#6-public-api-surfaces-public-apits-exports-secondary-entry-points-and-versioning))
- **exports map**: the generated `package.json` field that lists the only subpaths a consumer can import ([38 §6](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#6-public-api-surfaces-public-apits-exports-secondary-entry-points-and-versioning))
- **subpath (exports)**: a package-name suffix such as `ui-lib/testing` that an `exports` entry maps to a file ([38 §6](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#6-public-api-surfaces-public-apits-exports-secondary-entry-points-and-versioning))
- **secondary entry point**: a library sub-folder with its own `ng-package.json`, bundle and subpath ([38 §6](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#6-public-api-surfaces-public-apits-exports-secondary-entry-points-and-versioning))
- **bundled typings**: one `.d.ts` per entry point that holds only its exported names ([38 §6](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#6-public-api-surfaces-public-apits-exports-secondary-entry-points-and-versioning))
- **semantic versioning**: major.minor.patch numbers where a breaking change needs a new major ([38 §6](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#6-public-api-surfaces-public-apits-exports-secondary-entry-points-and-versioning))
- **deprecation**: marking an export with `@deprecated` for one major before it is removed ([38 §6](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#6-public-api-surfaces-public-apits-exports-secondary-entry-points-and-versioning))
- **monorepo**: one repository that holds many projects ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **Nx workspace**: a monorepo with Nx's project graph, tags, affected commands and cache on top ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **project graph**: the map of projects and the imports between them that Nx builds from code and configuration ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **tag**: a string on a project's configuration that dependency constraints select by ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **dependency constraint**: a rule that says which tags a tagged project may depend on ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **affected project**: a touched project or any project that reaches one through imports ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **computation cache**: stored task results that Nx replays when the task's inputs hash the same ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **task input**: a file or value whose change makes a task's hash change ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **task output**: a file a task produces, stored and restored on a cache hit ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **named input**: a reusable name for a set of task inputs, such as the default `{projectRoot}/**/*` ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **remote cache**: a computation cache shared between machines ([38 §7](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#7-nx-monorepos-the-project-graph-tags-nxenforce-module-boundaries-affected-commands-and-caching))
- **micro-frontend**: a separately built and deployed part of the UI, loaded by a host at run time ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **host (shell)**: the app that loads micro-frontends at run time ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **remote**: a separately deployed build that a host loads ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **exposed module**: a file a remote publishes under a key such as `./Component` ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **shared dependency**: a package host and remotes agree to load once instead of bundling each ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **singleton**: a shared dependency of which only one instance is loaded at run time ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **Module Federation**: a webpack (or rspack) runtime that loads remotes and negotiates shared packages ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **Native Federation**: Module Federation's model on ES modules and an import map, built with esbuild through the Angular application builder ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **modular monolith**: one deployable split into libraries with enforced boundaries ([38 §8](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#8-micro-frontends-module-federation-native-federation-and-when-they-are-justified))
- **formatter**: a tool that rewrites layout only, such as line breaks and quotes ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **linter**: a tool that reports code that is probably wrong or against a convention ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **flat config**: ESLint's `eslint.config.js`, an array of file patterns and rules ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **rule**: one named check of a linter, set to off, warn or error ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **processor**: a linter hook that hands code embedded in a file, such as an inline template, to other rules ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **pre-commit hook**: a script Git runs before it records a commit ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **staged files**: the files added with `git add` for the next commit ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **conventional commits**: commit messages shaped `<type>[optional scope]: <description>` ([38 §9](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#9-linting-and-formatting-angular-eslint-prettier-import-ordering-and-commit-hooks))
- **story**: one component rendered in one state, outside the app ([38 §10](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#10-storybook-for-component-development))
- **args**: Storybook's name for a component's inputs gathered in one object ([38 §10](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#10-storybook-for-component-development))
- **decorator (Storybook)**: a wrapper around a rendered story, not the TypeScript or Angular decorator syntax ([38 §10](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#10-storybook-for-component-development))
- **interaction test**: a story with a `play` function that simulates user actions ([38 §10](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#10-storybook-for-component-development))
- **visual regression test**: a test that compares screenshots with stored ones ([38 §10](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#10-storybook-for-component-development))
- **continuous integration (CI)**: a server that runs the same checks on every change ([38 §11](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#11-a-ci-pipeline-for-a-front-end-lint-test-build-end-to-end-and-deploy-previews))
- **pipeline stage**: one check in the CI run, such as lint or build ([38 §11](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#11-a-ci-pipeline-for-a-front-end-lint-test-build-end-to-end-and-deploy-previews))
- **gate**: the rule that a later stage starts only if the earlier one passed ([38 §11](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#11-a-ci-pipeline-for-a-front-end-lint-test-build-end-to-end-and-deploy-previews))
- **cache key (CI)**: the label a saved folder is stored under in CI, so the same key restores it ([38 §11](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#11-a-ci-pipeline-for-a-front-end-lint-test-build-end-to-end-and-deploy-previews))
- **artifact (CI)**: a named file set stored by the CI service and handed to a later stage ([38 §11](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#11-a-ci-pipeline-for-a-front-end-lint-test-build-end-to-end-and-deploy-previews))
- **deploy preview**: a build of one change published at its own address for review ([38 §11](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#11-a-ci-pipeline-for-a-front-end-lint-test-build-end-to-end-and-deploy-previews))
- **`npm ci`**: install exactly what the lockfile says, failing if it disagrees with `package.json` ([38 §11](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#11-a-ci-pipeline-for-a-front-end-lint-test-build-end-to-end-and-deploy-previews))
- **sample tree**: the lab's `sample-app/` folder, the smallest tree holding every folder kind of the module ([38 §12](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#12-a-complete-production-folder-tree-folder-by-folder))
- **layout shell**: the component that draws the frame (heading, outlet) around every feature ([38 §12](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#12-a-complete-production-folder-tree-folder-by-folder))
- **design token**: a design decision stored once as a CSS custom property ([38 §12](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#12-a-complete-production-folder-tree-folder-by-folder); owner [10 §6](../modules/10-css-essentials.md#6-custom-properties-theming-and-color-scheme))
- **build graph**: ng-packagr's internal map of nodes keyed by URL, where each entry point is a node and an edge means "imports" ([38 §13](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#13-under-the-hood-ng-packagrs-build-graph-and-how-nx-computes-affected-projects-and-task-hashes))
- **topological order**: an order in which every node comes after everything it depends on ([38 §13](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#13-under-the-hood-ng-packagrs-build-graph-and-how-nx-computes-affected-projects-and-task-hashes))
- **dependency cycle**: nodes that depend on each other (A needs B, B needs A), so no topological order exists ([38 §13](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#13-under-the-hood-ng-packagrs-build-graph-and-how-nx-computes-affected-projects-and-task-hashes))
- **task hash**: Nx's digest of a task's declared inputs and command, used as its cache key ([38 §13](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#13-under-the-hood-ng-packagrs-build-graph-and-how-nx-computes-affected-projects-and-task-hashes))
- **project alias**: the import name a project must be imported through, so a relative path into it counts as a violation ([Exercise 38.3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#ex38-3))
- **global input**: a file outside every project, such as the root `package.json`, whose change touches every project ([Exercise 38.4](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#ex38-4))
- **wave (task order)**: the tasks whose last dependency finished in the previous wave, so they could run in parallel ([Exercise 38.4](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#ex38-4))

## Module 39

- **AngularJS**: the 1.x framework, whose support officially ended in January 2022 ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **scope (AngularJS)**: a plain object that holds the model for a part of the page ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **`$rootScope`**: the one scope every other AngularJS scope descends from ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **watcher (AngularJS)**: a function that reads a value, plus a listener that runs when the value differs from the last one seen ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **digest**: the AngularJS loop that compares every watcher and repeats until a full pass changes nothing ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **dirty checking**: comparing every watched value with its last value after anything might have changed ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle); owner [21 §1](../modules/21-change-detection.md#1-the-synchronization-problem-dirty-checking-and-fine-grained-reactivity))
- **`$apply`**: runs your function, then a digest ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **two-way binding**: a template edit writes the model and a model change rewrites the template ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **isolate scope**: an AngularJS scope that does not inherit prototypally from its parent ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **TTL (digest)**: the digest's loop limit, 10 in AngularJS 1.8.3 ([39 §1](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#1-angularjs-scopes-watchers-and-the-digest-cycle))
- **rewrite (AngularJS to Angular)**: a new framework with no in-place upgrade path from the old one ([39 §2](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#2-why-angular-was-rewritten-and-what-carried-over))
- **one-way data flow**: property bindings carry values from component to DOM and event bindings from DOM to component, checked once per tick ([39 §2](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#2-why-angular-was-rewritten-and-what-carried-over); owner [13 §4](../modules/13-components-and-templates.md#4-property-attribute-class-and-style-bindings))
- **minification**: shrinking code by renaming locals (and stripping whitespace) ([39 §2](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#2-why-angular-was-rewritten-and-what-carried-over))
- **minification-safe DI annotation**: AngularJS dependency names listed as strings (`fn.$inject` or an array ending with the function), so renaming parameters does not break injection ([39 §2](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#2-why-angular-was-rewritten-and-what-carried-over))
- **ES module**: a file whose `import` and `export` declarations the engine links before running it ([39 §2](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#2-why-angular-was-rewritten-and-what-carried-over); owner [05 §1](../modules/05-js-modules-memory-modern-features.md#1-es-modules-static-structure-linking-and-live-bindings))
- **zone.js**: a library that patches the browser's async APIs so Angular knows when code finished ([39 §2](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#2-why-angular-was-rewritten-and-what-carried-over); owner [21 §3](../modules/21-change-detection.md#3-zonejs-and-ngzone-how-changes-used-to-be-detected-and-why-zone-left-the-default))
- **semantic versioning**: version numbers `major.minor.patch` where the level of the number says how much a release may change ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **active support**: the first 12 months of a major, with regularly scheduled updates and patches ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **LTS (long-term support)**: the 12 months after active support, with only critical fixes and security patches ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **deprecation period**: the time a deprecated API stays present, at least until the next major release, about 12 months ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **developer preview**: a working API not yet stabilized, outside the versioning and deprecation policies ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **experimental (API status)**: an API that might not become stable, outside the versioning and deprecation policies ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **`@deprecated` JSDoc tag**: a comment tag on a declaration that editors read to flag uses of it ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **compiler ABI**: the internal runtime API (`ɵɵ`-prefixed names) that the compiler's generated code calls ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy))
- **tree-shakeable provider**: a provider the bundler drops when nothing uses it ([39 §4](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#4-the-version-matrix-part-1-v2-to-v8))
- **`Renderer2`**: the service components call to change DOM nodes; it replaced `Renderer` ([39 §4](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#4-the-version-matrix-part-1-v2-to-v8))
- **AOT (ahead-of-time compiled) output**: the component code the compiler generates at build time ([39 §4](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#4-the-version-matrix-part-1-v2-to-v8))
- **string-based lazy loading**: a `loadChildren` string such as `path/to/module#ModuleName` that the build resolves ([39 §4](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#4-the-version-matrix-part-1-v2-to-v8))
- **differential loading**: building two bundles, one for modern browsers and one for older ones ([39 §4](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#4-the-version-matrix-part-1-v2-to-v8))
- **budget (bundle)**: a bundle size limit in the build configuration ([39 §4](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#4-the-version-matrix-part-1-v2-to-v8))
- **View Engine**: the compiler and runtime Angular used before Ivy became the default in v9 ([39 §5](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#5-the-version-matrix-part-2-v9-to-v15-from-ivy-to-standalone); owner [12 §8](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#8-under-the-hood-what-the-compiler-emits-and-what-the-runtime-does-with-it))
- **Ivy**: the compiler and runtime that replaced View Engine, the default in v9 and the only engine from v15 ([39 §5](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#5-the-version-matrix-part-2-v9-to-v15-from-ivy-to-standalone); owner [12 §8](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#8-under-the-hood-what-the-compiler-emits-and-what-the-runtime-does-with-it))
- **`ngcc`**: the Angular compatibility compiler, which made View Engine libraries usable in Ivy builds until v16 removed it ([39 §5](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#5-the-version-matrix-part-2-v9-to-v15-from-ivy-to-standalone))
- **partial compilation**: the library publishing format, an intermediate form that the application's build finishes ([39 §5](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#5-the-version-matrix-part-2-v9-to-v15-from-ivy-to-standalone); owner [12 §8](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#8-under-the-hood-what-the-compiler-emits-and-what-the-runtime-does-with-it))
- **Angular Package Format**: the layout `ng-packagr` writes for a library ([39 §5](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#5-the-version-matrix-part-2-v9-to-v15-from-ivy-to-standalone); owner [38 §5](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#5-angular-cli-libraries-ng-generate-library-ng-packagr-and-consuming-the-build))
- **MDC**: the `material-components-web` package that Angular Material's v15 components were re-implemented on ([39 §5](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#5-the-version-matrix-part-2-v9-to-v15-from-ivy-to-standalone))
- **default (for new projects)**: what `ng new` or the compiler does when you set nothing; existing projects keep their behavior until `ng update` or you change it ([39 §6](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#6-the-version-matrix-part-3-v16-to-v22-from-signals-to-onpush-by-default))
- **signal**: a value that tracks its readers ([39 §6](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#6-the-version-matrix-part-3-v16-to-v22-from-signals-to-onpush-by-default); owner [17 §1](../modules/17-signals.md#1-why-signals-exist))
- **zoneless**: change detection that runs without zone.js ([39 §6](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#6-the-version-matrix-part-3-v16-to-v22-from-signals-to-onpush-by-default); owner [21 §5](../modules/21-change-detection.md#5-zoneless-change-detection-the-scheduler-its-triggers-and-pendingtasks))
- **OnPush**: a strategy that checks a component only when something marks it dirty ([39 §6](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#6-the-version-matrix-part-3-v16-to-v22-from-signals-to-onpush-by-default); owner [21 §2](../modules/21-change-detection.md#2-onpush-and-eager-strategies-and-what-marks-a-view-dirty))
- **Signal Forms**: forms built from a model signal ([39 §6](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#6-the-version-matrix-part-3-v16-to-v22-from-signals-to-onpush-by-default); owner [26 §1](../modules/26-signal-forms.md#1-why-a-third-forms-api-model-first-signals-end-to-end))
- **application builder**: the `application` builder, today `@angular/build:application`, that `ng new` has used since v17 ([39 §6](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#6-the-version-matrix-part-3-v16-to-v22-from-signals-to-onpush-by-default); owner [12 §3](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#3-building-the-application-builder-output-layout-and-budgets))
- **migration (ng update)**: a schematic that rewrites your code for one version step ([39 §7](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#7-updating-in-practice-ng-update-migration-schematics-and-the-update-guide); owner [12 §2](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#2-the-cli-and-the-workspace-angularjson-projects-schematics-and-ng-update))
- **peer dependency**: a version range a library lists for a package it expects you to provide ([39 §7](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#7-updating-in-practice-ng-update-migration-schematics-and-the-update-guide))
- **package group**: the packages released and updated together, listed in a package's `ng-update.packageGroup` field ([39 §7](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#7-updating-in-practice-ng-update-migration-schematics-and-the-update-guide); owner [12 §1](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#1-what-a-framework-does-and-angulars-philosophy))
- **required versus optional migration**: a required migration runs unasked during `ng update`; an optional one is offered in a prompt, pre-selected only when `recommended` ([39 §7](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#7-updating-in-practice-ng-update-migration-schematics-and-the-update-guide))
- **Update Guide**: the update.angular.dev page that turns a from-version, a to-version and an app level into a before, during and after checklist ([39 §7](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#7-updating-in-practice-ng-update-migration-schematics-and-the-update-guide))
- **hop**: one `ng update` from one major to the next; a version can be skipped in the calendar but not in the update ([39 §8](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#8-skipping-versions-safely-one-major-per-hop-node-and-typescript-ranges-and-libraries-that-block-a-hop); owner of the mechanics [12 §2](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#2-the-cli-and-the-workspace-angularjson-projects-schematics-and-ng-update))
- **compatibility table**: angular.dev's "Version compatibility" page, listing the Node.js, TypeScript and RxJS each Angular minor requires ([39 §8](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#8-skipping-versions-safely-one-major-per-hop-node-and-typescript-ranges-and-libraries-that-block-a-hop))
- **blocking library**: a dependency that cannot be installed beside the next Angular major, because its peer range excludes it or it cannot run there ([39 §8](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#8-skipping-versions-safely-one-major-per-hop-node-and-typescript-ranges-and-libraries-that-block-a-hop))
- **hybrid application**: a page in which AngularJS and Angular both run, each owning its own DOM elements ([39 §9](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#9-from-angularjs-to-angular-ngupgrade-hybrids-the-strangler-fig-and-rewrites))
- **ngUpgrade**: the `@angular/upgrade/static` package that builds a hybrid application ([39 §9](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#9-from-angularjs-to-angular-ngupgrade-hybrids-the-strangler-fig-and-rewrites))
- **downgrade (ngUpgrade)**: wrap an Angular component or service so AngularJS can use it ([39 §9](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#9-from-angularjs-to-angular-ngupgrade-hybrids-the-strangler-fig-and-rewrites))
- **upgrade (ngUpgrade)**: wrap an AngularJS component or service so Angular can use it ([39 §9](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#9-from-angularjs-to-angular-ngupgrade-hybrids-the-strangler-fig-and-rewrites))
- **strangler fig**: a migration that grows a new system around the old one and moves traffic to it route by route until the old one can be removed ([39 §9](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#9-from-angularjs-to-angular-ngupgrade-hybrids-the-strangler-fig-and-rewrites))
- **reverse proxy**: a server in front of several apps that forwards each request to one of them by path ([39 §9](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#9-from-angularjs-to-angular-ngupgrade-hybrids-the-strangler-fig-and-rewrites))
- **cut-over**: the moment all traffic moves from the old system to the new one ([39 §9](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#9-from-angularjs-to-angular-ngupgrade-hybrids-the-strangler-fig-and-rewrites))
- **migration program**: many migrations run as small changes in a planned order, instead of one pull request ([39 §10](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#10-a-migration-program-for-an-old-angular-codebase-order-dependencies-and-checkpoints))
- **dependency edge**: a rule that one migration must finish before another starts, because the later one needs its result ([39 §10](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#10-a-migration-program-for-an-old-angular-codebase-order-dependencies-and-checkpoints))
- **track (migration)**: an independent sequence of migrations with its own pull requests ([39 §10](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#10-a-migration-program-for-an-old-angular-codebase-order-dependencies-and-checkpoints))
- **checkpoint**: the build, tests, render check and commit that follow each migration batch ([39 §10](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#10-a-migration-program-for-an-old-angular-codebase-order-dependencies-and-checkpoints))
- **codemod**: a program that rewrites source code; a schematic is the CLI's codemod ([39 §10](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#10-a-migration-program-for-an-old-angular-codebase-order-dependencies-and-checkpoints); owner [18 §8](../modules/18-control-flow-and-defer.md#8-migrating-with-ng-generate-angularcorecontrol-flow))
- **standalone component**: a component that lists its own imports instead of being declared in an NgModule ([39 §10](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#10-a-migration-program-for-an-old-angular-codebase-order-dependencies-and-checkpoints); owner [15 §2](../modules/15-standalone-and-ngmodules.md#2-standalone-components-and-the-imports-array))
- **behavior-preserving migration**: a migration that rewrites your code so the program behaves the same under the new version, usually by writing an old default back ([39 §11](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#11-the-v22-upgrade-what-ng-update-changes-and-why-each-migration-keeps-the-old-behavior))
- **opt-out provider**: a feature passed to a provider function to switch a default off, such as `withNoIncrementalHydration()` ([39 §11](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#11-the-v22-upgrade-what-ng-update-changes-and-why-each-migration-keeps-the-old-behavior))
- **compiler marker function**: a call in source that the compiler rewrites and that does not exist at runtime, such as `$safeNavigationMigration(…)` ([39 §11](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#11-the-v22-upgrade-what-ng-update-changes-and-why-each-migration-keeps-the-old-behavior))
- **semver range**: a condition on version numbers, such as `>21.2.3 <=22.2.1`, that a version satisfies or not ([39 §12](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ng-update-selects-migrations-and-how-the-update-guide-builds-its-checklist))
- **prerelease (version)**: a build before a release, such as `22.0.0-next.1`, ordered below `22.0.0` ([39 §12](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ng-update-selects-migrations-and-how-the-update-guide-builds-its-checklist))
- **recommended migration**: an optional migration flagged `recommended`, pre-selected in `ng update`'s prompt ([39 §12](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ng-update-selects-migrations-and-how-the-update-guide-builds-its-checklist))
- **`possibleIn`**: in the Update Guide's data, the first version in which a step's change can be made ([39 §12](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ng-update-selects-migrations-and-how-the-update-guide-builds-its-checklist))
- **`necessaryAsOf`**: in the Update Guide's data, the version from which a step's change is required ([39 §12](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#12-under-the-hood-how-ng-update-selects-migrations-and-how-the-update-guide-builds-its-checklist))

## Module 40

- **UI library**: code that renders components into a root you give it and leaves routing, data, forms, build and tests to you ([40 §1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#1-library-framework-progressive-framework-what-each-project-decides-for-you))
- **framework**: code that owns the entry point and the update loop and calls your code (owner [12 §1](../modules/12-angular-how-it-works.md#1-what-a-framework-does-and-angulars-philosophy); [40 §1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#1-library-framework-progressive-framework-what-each-project-decides-for-you))
- **progressive framework**: a framework that can be dropped into a page as a library and grows by adding official pieces ([40 §1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#1-library-framework-progressive-framework-what-each-project-decides-for-you))
- **meta-framework**: a framework built on top of a UI library, such as Next.js on React ([40 §1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#1-library-framework-progressive-framework-what-each-project-decides-for-you))
- **compiler-first framework**: a framework that moves much of its work to build time ([40 §1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#1-library-framework-progressive-framework-what-each-project-decides-for-you))
- **first-party package**: a package the project's own team maintains ([40 §1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#1-library-framework-progressive-framework-what-each-project-decides-for-you))
- **template**: a restricted language the compiler parses ahead of time, so it knows which nodes are static and which bindings can change ([40 §2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#2-templates-jsx-and-single-file-components-what-the-compiler-knows))
- **render function**: a function that returns a description of the UI ([40 §2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#2-templates-jsx-and-single-file-components-what-the-compiler-knows))
- **JSX**: a syntax extension of JavaScript that compiles to function calls ([40 §2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#2-templates-jsx-and-single-file-components-what-the-compiler-knows))
- **single-file component (SFC)**: a `.vue` or `.svelte` file that keeps template, script and style together ([40 §2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#2-templates-jsx-and-single-file-components-what-the-compiler-knows))
- **patch flag**: a number Vue's compiler attaches to a node to say which parts can change ([40 §2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#2-templates-jsx-and-single-file-components-what-the-compiler-knows))
- **static hoisting**: the compiler marking a constant element so the renderer reuses it ([40 §2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#2-templates-jsx-and-single-file-components-what-the-compiler-knows))
- **component state (React)**: data a component owns through a hook like `useState` ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **re-render**: React calling a component function again, and by default every component it returns ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **reconciliation**: pairing the new element tree with the old, keeping a child with the same type and key and replacing any other ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **key**: a child's identity among its siblings; reconciliation keeps the instance and state of a child whose key and type match ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **commit**: the phase that writes the differences between renders to the DOM ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **bailout**: React skipping work: an `Object.is`-equal state update, or a component whose props and context did not change ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **memo (React)**: a wrapper that skips a component's re-render when its new props are shallow-equal to the old ones ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **shallow equality**: same keys, each value equal by `Object.is` ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **context (React)**: a value a provider hands to the descendants that read it, without props ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **React Compiler**: the Babel plugin `babel-plugin-react-compiler` that inserts the equivalent of manual memoization at build time ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **memo cache**: the per-instance array (`useMemoCache`, internal) in which compiled React code keeps each memoized block's dependencies and results ([40 §3](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#3-reacts-model-re-rendering-reconciliation-memoization-and-the-react-compiler))
- **signal**: a value container that records who reads it and notifies them on write (owner [17 §1](../modules/17-signals.md#1-why-signals-exist); [40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **ref (Vue)**: Vue's signal-like container, read and written through `.value` ([40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **rune (Svelte)**: a Svelte 5 keyword such as `$state` ([40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **render effect**: a computation that writes to the DOM and re-runs when what it read changes ([40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **granularity**: the unit a write re-runs: a view, a component or one DOM expression ([40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **fine-grained reactivity**: reactivity whose unit of update is small: one view in Angular, a single DOM expression in Solid ([40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **`reactive` Proxy (Vue)**: Vue's deep wrapper that tracks property access (owner [03 §5](../modules/03-js-objects-prototypes-classes.md#5-proxy-and-reflect); [40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **resumability (Qwik)**: pausing execution on the server and resuming it on the client without replaying the application logic ([40 §4](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#4-signals-beyond-angular-vue-refs-solid-svelte-runes-preact-and-qwik))
- **provider (React)**: the element `<SomeContext value={…}>` that hands one value to the components below it that read the context; it builds nothing ([40 §5](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#5-dependency-injection-and-shared-state-injectors-context-and-provideinject))
- **`provide`/`inject` (Vue)**: functions that store a value under a key in a component and read it from the nearest ancestor that stored one ([40 §5](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#5-dependency-injection-and-shared-state-injectors-context-and-provideinject))
- **data-fetching library**: a library that loads and caches server data for components, such as `@tanstack/react-query` ([40 §6](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#6-routing-forms-data-fetching-tooling-and-testing-first-party-or-ecosystem))
- **test runner**: the program that finds and runs your tests, such as Vitest ([40 §6](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#6-routing-forms-data-fetching-tooling-and-testing-first-party-or-ecosystem))
- **peer dependency**: a package the consumer installs, shared rather than bundled; its version range states which core versions a package accepts (owner [39 §7](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#7-updating-in-practice-ng-update-migration-schematics-and-the-update-guide); [40 §6](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#6-routing-forms-data-fetching-tooling-and-testing-first-party-or-ecosystem))
- **download count**: npm's tally of tarball downloads of a package in a period, including build servers and mirrors; not users ([40 §7](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#7-ecosystem-size-release-policies-and-breaking-changes))
- **transitive dependency**: a dependency of a dependency, so one install can download many packages ([40 §7](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#7-ecosystem-size-release-policies-and-breaking-changes))
- **end of life**: the date a version stops receiving new features, updates and fixes, such as Vue 2 on December 31st, 2023 ([40 §7](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#7-ecosystem-size-release-policies-and-breaking-changes))
- **deprecation policy**: a project's statement of how long a deprecated feature keeps working before removal; Angular's is owner [39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy), Vue's "removed in the next major release" ([40 §7](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#7-ecosystem-size-release-policies-and-breaking-changes))
- **must-have gate**: a requirement an option passes or fails, removing a failing option before any scoring ([40 §8](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#8-choosing-a-framework-balanced-trade-offs-and-how-to-answer-why-angular))
- **weighted criterion**: a property every remaining option is scored on, with a weight the team sets before scoring ([40 §8](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#8-choosing-a-framework-balanced-trade-offs-and-how-to-answer-why-angular))
- **sensitivity analysis**: checking which plausible weight change would flip the winner of a decision ([40 §8](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#8-choosing-a-framework-balanced-trade-offs-and-how-to-answer-why-angular))
- **decision record**: a written record of a choice's gates, weights, sourced scores and deciding assumption ([40 §8](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#8-choosing-a-framework-balanced-trade-offs-and-how-to-answer-why-angular))
- **portable domain core**: framework-free application state and rules that each framework reads through an adapter ([40 §8](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#8-choosing-a-framework-balanced-trade-offs-and-how-to-answer-why-angular))
- **adapter**: a thin layer that gives a core or a source the shape one consumer needs (owner [38 §3](../modules/38-architecture-and-production-structure.md#3-facades-and-the-data-access-layer); [40 §8](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#8-choosing-a-framework-balanced-trade-offs-and-how-to-answer-why-angular))
- **external store (React)**: state held outside React, read in a component with `useSyncExternalStore` ([40 §8](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#8-choosing-a-framework-balanced-trade-offs-and-how-to-answer-why-angular))
- **notification (reactivity)**: what a state write does to tell the runtime that work is owed ([40 §9](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#9-under-the-hood-one-state-change-traced-through-angular-react-vue-and-solid))
- **scheduling**: the runtime's decision of when the follow-up work runs: a tick, a microtask, a scheduler task or synchronously ([40 §9](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#9-under-the-hood-one-state-change-traced-through-angular-react-vue-and-solid))
- **unit of re-execution**: the code that runs again after a write: a view, a component subtree, a component or one expression ([40 §9](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#9-under-the-hood-one-state-change-traced-through-angular-react-vue-and-solid))
- **trace table**: one change followed through several runtimes in the columns notify, schedule, re-run, compare and write ([40 §9](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#9-under-the-hood-one-state-change-traced-through-angular-react-vue-and-solid))
- **virtual DOM**: a tree of plain objects that render functions return; the runtime diffs each new tree against the old and writes only the differences to the DOM ([40 Exercise 40.1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#ex40-1))
- **vnode**: one node of a virtual DOM (an element, a text or a component) that keeps the DOM node it was mounted with ([40 Exercise 40.1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#ex40-1))
- **longest increasing subsequence**: the longest run of children whose old positions already increase; Vue's keyed diff leaves it in place and moves the rest ([40 Exercise 40.1](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#ex40-1))
- **weighted sum**: a score per candidate made by multiplying each criterion's score by its weight and adding the products; shown divided by the weight total ([40 Exercise 40.2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#ex40-2))
- **Borda count**: a ranking method in which each criterion gives a candidate one point per candidate scored strictly lower and half a point per tie, multiplied by the weight, so only order counts ([40 Exercise 40.2](../modules/40-framework-comparisons.md#ex40-2))

## Module 41

- **`CorsConfigurationSource`**: Spring's object that maps URL patterns to `CorsConfiguration` rules; Spring MVC and Spring Security read the same one ([41 §1](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#1-cors-on-both-sides-springs-corsconfigurationsource-spring-security-preflight-caching-credentials-and-the-dev-proxy))
- **origin pattern**: an allowed-origin entry with a wildcard host, such as `https://*.example.com`, set through `setAllowedOriginPatterns`; the alternative to `"*"` when credentials are allowed ([41 §1](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#1-cors-on-both-sides-springs-corsconfigurationsource-spring-security-preflight-caching-credentials-and-the-dev-proxy))
- **exposed header**: a response header listed in `Access-Control-Expose-Headers`, so cross-origin script may read it beyond the CORS-safelisted ones ([41 §1](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#1-cors-on-both-sides-springs-corsconfigurationsource-spring-security-preflight-caching-credentials-and-the-dev-proxy))
- **CORS-safelisted header**: one of the seven response headers (such as `Content-Type`) that cross-origin script may read without being listed ([41 §1](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#1-cors-on-both-sides-springs-corsconfigurationsource-spring-security-preflight-caching-credentials-and-the-dev-proxy))
- **dev-server proxy**: `ng serve` forwarding matching paths (such as `/api/**`) to the API, so the browser sees one origin in development ([41 §1](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#1-cors-on-both-sides-springs-corsconfigurationsource-spring-security-preflight-caching-credentials-and-the-dev-proxy))
- **reverse proxy**: a server in front of both the app and the API that serves them under one origin in production (owner [44](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md); [41 §1](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#1-cors-on-both-sides-springs-corsconfigurationsource-spring-security-preflight-caching-credentials-and-the-dev-proxy))
- **DTO (data transfer object)**: a class made only for the wire, separate from the entity and filled by a mapper at the controller edge ([41 §2](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#2-dto-contracts-what-crosses-the-wire-absent-versus-null-and-checking-responses-at-the-boundary))
- **entity (persistence)**: the class mapped to a database table, such as a JPA entity; returning it exposes every column ([41 §2](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#2-dto-contracts-what-crosses-the-wire-absent-versus-null-and-checking-responses-at-the-boundary))
- **mapper**: code that copies an entity's fields into a DTO at the controller edge ([41 §2](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#2-dto-contracts-what-crosses-the-wire-absent-versus-null-and-checking-responses-at-the-boundary))
- **contract**: the JSON shape both sides agree on, not either side's class ([41 §2](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#2-dto-contracts-what-crosses-the-wire-absent-versus-null-and-checking-responses-at-the-boundary))
- **tolerant reader**: a reader that ignores fields it does not know; Jackson 3 does by default ([41 §2](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#2-dto-contracts-what-crosses-the-wire-absent-versus-null-and-checking-responses-at-the-boundary))
- **JSON Merge Patch**: RFC 7386's patch format: a member left out stays unchanged and a `null` member means removal ([41 §2](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#2-dto-contracts-what-crosses-the-wire-absent-versus-null-and-checking-responses-at-the-boundary))
- **springdoc**: springdoc-openapi, the library that reads a Spring app's controllers and DTOs and publishes its OpenAPI description at `/v3/api-docs` ([41 §3](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#3-openapi-with-springdoc-and-generated-typescript-clients-openapi-generator-orval-and-ng-openapi-gen))
- **OpenAPI description**: a JSON (or YAML) document of an API's paths and schemas; springdoc 3.1.1 writes OpenAPI 3.1.0 by default ([41 §3](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#3-openapi-with-springdoc-and-generated-typescript-clients-openapi-generator-orval-and-ng-openapi-gen))
- **code generator**: a tool that turns an OpenAPI description into TypeScript models plus request code ([41 §3](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#3-openapi-with-springdoc-and-generated-typescript-clients-openapi-generator-orval-and-ng-openapi-gen))
- **generated client**: the models and request functions or services a code generator emits; kept behind a data-access facade ([41 §3](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#3-openapi-with-springdoc-and-generated-typescript-clients-openapi-generator-orval-and-ng-openapi-gen))
- **facade**: one service in front of generated code, so components depend on it and not on the generator's output ([41 §3](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#3-openapi-with-springdoc-and-generated-typescript-clients-openapi-generator-orval-and-ng-openapi-gen))
- **drift check**: a build step that compares the client's schemas with the API description and fails on disagreement ([41 §3](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#3-openapi-with-springdoc-and-generated-typescript-clients-openapi-generator-orval-and-ng-openapi-gen))
- **provider verification**: the backend replaying a consumer's recorded contract interactions in its own build (owner of consumer-driven contracts: [30 §5](../modules/30-e2e-and-testing-strategy.md#5-api-mocking-at-the-network-layer-and-where-contract-tests-take-over); [41 §3](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#3-openapi-with-springdoc-and-generated-typescript-clients-openapi-generator-orval-and-ng-openapi-gen))
- **backward-compatible change**: a change after which every deployed client still works, such as an optional field or a new endpoint ([41 §4](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#4-evolving-the-contract-compatible-changes-api-versioning-in-spring-framework-7-and-deprecation-headers))
- **breaking change**: a change that stops some deployed client working: removing or renaming, optional becoming required, a narrower type, a new enum value a closed enum rejects ([41 §4](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#4-evolving-the-contract-compatible-changes-api-versioning-in-spring-framework-7-and-deprecation-headers))
- **API version**: the value a request carries (header, query parameter, path segment or media type parameter) that names the contract it expects ([41 §4](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#4-evolving-the-contract-compatible-changes-api-versioning-in-spring-framework-7-and-deprecation-headers))
- **baseline version**: Spring's `"1.2+"` mapping, which matches 1.2 and the supported versions above it ([41 §4](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#4-evolving-the-contract-compatible-changes-api-versioning-in-spring-framework-7-and-deprecation-headers))
- **deprecation header**: the `Deprecation` response header (RFC 9745), whose value is a Date such as `@1688169599` ([41 §4](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#4-evolving-the-contract-compatible-changes-api-versioning-in-spring-framework-7-and-deprecation-headers))
- **sunset**: the `Sunset` response header (RFC 8594) saying when a resource is expected to stop responding ([41 §4](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#4-evolving-the-contract-compatible-changes-api-versioning-in-spring-framework-7-and-deprecation-headers))
- **problem detail**: RFC 9457's JSON shape for an HTTP error, sent as `application/problem+json`, with the members `type`, `title`, `status`, `detail` and `instance` ([41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **problem type URI**: the `type` member of a problem detail, the identifier clients key on; when absent it is `about:blank`, meaning nothing beyond the HTTP status ([41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **extension member**: a member a problem type adds beyond the standard five, such as an `errors` list; clients ignore the ones they do not recognize ([41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **JSON Pointer**: RFC 6901's string syntax for one value inside a JSON document, such as `#/quantity` in its URI-fragment form ([41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **`ResponseEntityExceptionHandler`**: Spring MVC's base class for a `@ControllerAdvice` that renders Spring MVC's exceptions as problem details ([41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **interceptor**: a function every `HttpClient` request and response passes through (owner [27 §4](../modules/27-http-client.md#4-interceptors-the-functional-chain-order-and-next-and-class-interceptors-through-withinterceptorsfromdi); [41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **conditional request**: a request that carries a precondition, such as `If-Match` with the `ETag` the client read, so the server can refuse it with 412 (`ETag`: [09 §4](../modules/09-web-networking-storage-security.md#4-http-caching); [41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **lost update**: one client's write overwriting a newer change it never saw ([41 §5](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#5-error-contracts-with-problemdetail-rfc-9457-and-mapping-them-in-angular))
- **offset pagination**: asking for a page by number and size, usually with a total count; it can jump to any page but shifts under inserts ([41 §6](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#6-pagination-sorting-and-filtering-offset-versus-cursor-and-how-spring-data-serializes-a-page))
- **cursor (keyset) pagination**: asking for the items after a position taken from the last item seen; stable under inserts, but no page jumps and usually no total ([41 §6](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#6-pagination-sorting-and-filtering-offset-versus-cursor-and-how-spring-data-serializes-a-page))
- **stable sort**: a sort whose order is the same on every request, so pages and cursors do not repeat or skip items ([41 §6](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#6-pagination-sorting-and-filtering-offset-versus-cursor-and-how-spring-data-serializes-a-page))
- **tiebreaker**: a unique last sort key, such as `id`, that orders rows whose other sort keys are equal ([41 §6](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#6-pagination-sorting-and-filtering-offset-versus-cursor-and-how-spring-data-serializes-a-page))
- **`PagedModel`**: Spring Data's DTO for a `Page`, rendering `content` and a `page` metadata object, instead of the domain type `PageImpl` ([41 §6](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#6-pagination-sorting-and-filtering-offset-versus-cursor-and-how-spring-data-serializes-a-page))
- **`Window`**: Spring Data's result of one scroll step: a chunk of items from which a scroll position for the next step is taken ([41 §6](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#6-pagination-sorting-and-filtering-offset-versus-cursor-and-how-spring-data-serializes-a-page))
- **scroll position**: Spring Data's `ScrollPosition`, the place in a query result after which the next scroll step starts (the position is exclusive) ([41 §6](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#6-pagination-sorting-and-filtering-offset-versus-cursor-and-how-spring-data-serializes-a-page))
- **Bean Validation**: Jakarta Validation, the Java standard for declaring constraints on an object's fields and checking them at run time ([41 §7](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#7-validation-on-both-layers-bean-validation-angular-validators-and-server-errors-on-form-fields))
- **constraint annotation**: a Bean Validation annotation, such as `@NotBlank`, `@Size` or `@Email`, that states one rule on a field or parameter ([41 §7](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#7-validation-on-both-layers-bean-validation-angular-validators-and-server-errors-on-form-fields))
- **syntactic rule**: a validation rule decidable from the value alone, such as required, length or pattern, which a client can mirror ([41 §7](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#7-validation-on-both-layers-bean-validation-angular-validators-and-server-errors-on-form-fields))
- **semantic rule**: a validation rule that needs server data, such as uniqueness or stock, so only the server can decide it ([41 §7](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#7-validation-on-both-layers-bean-validation-angular-validators-and-server-errors-on-form-fields))
- **method validation**: Spring MVC's validation of constraints declared directly on a controller method's parameters, which raises `HandlerMethodValidationException` ([41 §7](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#7-validation-on-both-layers-bean-validation-angular-validators-and-server-errors-on-form-fields))
- **server error on a field**: a validation error the server returns for one field, shown under that field's control ([26 §7](../modules/26-signal-forms.md#7-submission-submit-formroot-submitting-and-server-errors); [41 §7](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#7-validation-on-both-layers-bean-validation-angular-validators-and-server-errors-on-form-fields))
- **multipart/form-data**: an HTTP request body that carries several parts, such as a file and its metadata, each with its own headers ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
- **part**: one section of a multipart body, with its own headers such as `Content-Disposition` ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
- **chunked upload**: sending a file as slices, each with its byte range, instead of one request ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
