- **resumable upload**: a chunked upload whose server reports what it holds, so a failure resends only the rest ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
- **`Content-Range`**: the header that names the byte range of a body and the complete length, such as `bytes 0-4/12` (RFC 9110 §14.4) ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
- **`Content-Disposition`**: the header that tells the recipient to show a body inline or save it as an attachment, and under which file name (RFC 6266) ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
- **`filename*`**: the `Content-Disposition` parameter that carries the file name as charset'language'percent-encoded text, for non-Latin names (RFC 5987) ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
- **streaming response**: a response whose body the server writes while it is produced, such as Spring's `StreamingResponseBody` ([41 §8](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#8-files-multipart-and-chunked-uploads-progress-and-downloads-with-content-disposition-and-streaming))
- **instant**: one moment in time, the same for everyone, such as Java's `Instant`; on the wire, ISO 8601 text ending in `Z` or an offset ([41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **calendar date**: a day with no time and no zone, such as a birthday or Java's `LocalDate`; on the wire, `YYYY-MM-DD` ([41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **offset**: the distance from UTC written after a time, such as `+02:00`; `Z` means zero ([41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **IANA time zone**: a region name from the IANA time zone database, such as `Europe/Madrid`, whose offset changes with daylight saving ([41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **ISO 8601**: the international notation for dates and times, such as `2026-10-21T03:30:00Z` ([41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **ISO 4217 currency code**: the three-letter code of a currency, such as `EUR` or `JPY` ([41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **minor units**: the smallest unit of a currency counted as an integer, such as cents ([34 §10](../modules/34-i18n.md#10-dates-times-numbers-and-money-time-zones-currency-codes-and-minor-units); [41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **decimal string**: an amount written as text, such as `"19.90"`, so that no JSON number turns it into a double ([41 §9](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#9-dates-time-zones-decimals-and-money-across-the-wire))
- **trace table (request path)**: a list of one request's hops, each with what it reads and what it can return ([41 §10](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#10-under-the-hood-one-failed-request-through-springs-filters-and-handlers-and-angulars-backend))
- **filter chain**: the servlet filters a request passes before the servlet, each able to answer and stop it, such as Spring Security's ([41 §10](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#10-under-the-hood-one-failed-request-through-springs-filters-and-handlers-and-angulars-backend))
- **`DispatcherServlet`**: Spring MVC's servlet, which finds the handler, invokes it and asks the exception resolvers when it throws ([41 §10](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#10-under-the-hood-one-failed-request-through-springs-filters-and-handlers-and-angulars-backend))
- **handler mapping**: the Spring MVC strategy that picks the controller method for a request, such as `RequestMappingHandlerMapping` ([41 §10](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#10-under-the-hood-one-failed-request-through-springs-filters-and-handlers-and-angulars-backend))
- **argument resolver**: the Spring MVC strategy that builds one controller-method parameter, such as `RequestResponseBodyMethodProcessor` for `@RequestBody` ([41 §10](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#10-under-the-hood-one-failed-request-through-springs-filters-and-handlers-and-angulars-backend))
- **exception resolver**: a Spring MVC `HandlerExceptionResolver`, which turns an exception from mapping or a handler into a response ([41 §10](../modules/41-fullstack-api-contracts.md#10-under-the-hood-one-failed-request-through-springs-filters-and-handlers-and-angulars-backend))

## Module 42

- **authentication**: who is calling a service; it comes before authorization ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **authorization**: what a caller, once identified, may do ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **server session**: a login the server keeps as a record, with the browser holding only the session id ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **session id**: the key to a server session, held by the browser in a cookie and sent with every request ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **ambient credential**: a credential the browser attaches by itself, such as a cookie, so a page on another site can trigger a request that carries it ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **sticky session**: load-balancer routing that sends one client to the same instance every time, so that instance's memory holds the client's session ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **self-contained token**: a token that carries its own claims and a signature, so any instance can check it without a lookup, such as a JWT ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **JWT**: JSON Web Token: claims and a signature (or encryption) written as base64url text in dot-separated parts ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **claim**: a statement about the caller inside a token, such as `sub` (who) or `exp` (when the token expires) ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **signature**: proof that the holder of the issuer's key wrote a token's header and payload; a changed byte fails the check ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **base64url**: base64 with a URL-safe alphabet and no padding; encoding, not encryption ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **revocation**: ending a credential before it expires ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **deny list**: stored ids of tokens the server refuses even though they have not expired ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **Spring Session**: the Spring library that keeps HTTP sessions in a shared store such as Redis or a database table, so every instance reads them ([42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **OAuth 2.0**: a framework in which an authorization server issues an access token that a client uses at a resource server ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **client**: the application that asks for access on a user's behalf, here the Angular app ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **authorization server (IdP)**: the server that authenticates the user and issues tokens; the identity provider ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **resource server**: the API that accepts access tokens ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **access token**: the credential the API accepts, sent as a bearer token ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **OpenID Connect**: an identity layer on OAuth 2.0 that adds the ID token ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **ID token**: a JWT with claims about the login, meant for the client, not the API ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **public client**: a client that cannot keep a secret, such as a browser app ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **confidential client**: a client that can keep a secret, such as a Spring server ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **authorization code**: the short-lived value the IdP returns to the redirect URI and the app exchanges for tokens ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **redirect URI**: the callback address where the IdP sends the user back ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **PKCE**: Proof Key for Code Exchange: binds an authorization code to the client that started the flow ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **code_verifier**: the random secret the page keeps and sends only to the token endpoint ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **code_challenge**: the hash of the verifier sent with the authorization request ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **S256**: the PKCE method that hashes the verifier with SHA-256 and base64url-encodes it ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **state (OAuth)**: a random request value returned unchanged, tying the callback to the request ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **nonce (OpenID Connect)**: a random value copied into the ID token, tying it to the login ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **mix-up attack**: tricking a client into sending a code to the wrong authorization server; the `iss` parameter exposes it ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **implicit flow**: the retired flow that returned the access token in the redirect URL ([42 §2](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#2-oauth-20-and-openid-connect-for-a-single-page-app-authorization-code-with-pkce-and-why-the-implicit-flow-is-gone))
- **backend-for-frontend (BFF)**: a server on the app's site that is the OAuth client, keeps every token and proxies API calls; the browser holds only a session cookie ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **session cookie**: the cookie carrying a server session's id ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **token-mediating backend**: a server that runs the OAuth flow as a confidential client and hands the page the access token ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **browser-based OAuth client**: a single-page app that runs the whole OAuth flow and holds every token itself ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **authorized client**: Spring's record of a client registration, a user and the tokens issued to them ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **token relay**: a gateway or BFF adding the signed-in user's access token to the call it forwards ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **client hijacking**: injected script using the open page to call the API as the user, without stealing a token ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **authentication entry point**: what Spring Security runs to answer an unauthenticated request, such as a redirect to login or a 401 ([42 §3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#3-where-the-oauth-client-runs-a-browser-only-client-a-token-mediating-backend-or-a-backend-for-frontend-with-spring))
- **refresh token rotation**: the authorization server issuing a new refresh token on every use and invalidating the previous one ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **token family**: the chain of refresh tokens issued from one login through rotation ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **reuse detection**: the authorization server remembering used refresh tokens, so that one presented again revokes the family's active token ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **sender-constrained token**: a token bound to a key the client holds, so a copy without the key is useless ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **DPoP**: OAuth 2.0 Demonstrating Proof of Possession (RFC 9449), one way to sender-constrain tokens ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **silent renew**: rerunning the authorization request in a hidden iframe on the identity provider's session cookie to get new tokens without user interaction ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **Web Locks API**: a browser API for named locks that only one script of an origin can hold at a time, across its tabs and workers ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **Baseline**: MDN's label for a web feature's support across the core browsers ([42 §4](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#4-refresh-tokens-rotation-reuse-detection-silent-renew-and-refreshing-across-tabs))
- **JWKS (JSON Web Key Set)**: the set of public keys an issuer publishes so that resource servers can verify its tokens' signatures ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **key id (`kid`)**: a token header value naming which key in a JWKS signed it, used during key rollover ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **algorithm allow-list**: the signature algorithms (`alg` values) a token verifier accepts; any other, including `none`, is refused ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **clock skew**: the tolerated difference between the issuer's and the verifier's clocks when checking `exp` and `nbf`; 60 seconds in Spring Security ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **issuer (`iss`)**: the claim naming the authorization server that issued a token ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **audience (`aud`)**: the claim naming the APIs a token is intended for ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **scope (OAuth)**: a permission granted to the client, carried in a token's `scope` or `scp` claim ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **authority**: a permission string Spring Security checks, such as `SCOPE_orders:read` or `ROLE_admin` ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **`SCOPE_` prefix**: the prefix Spring Security's default converter puts before each scope to make an authority ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **opaque token**: a token with no readable claims, only a reference the authorization server can look up ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **token introspection**: a resource server asking the authorization server whether a token is active and what it grants (RFC 7662) ([42 §5](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#5-spring-security-as-a-resource-server-validating-jwts-and-turning-scopes-into-authorities))
- **CSRF token**: a secret the server expects back in a header or form field, which a forging site cannot read ([42 §6](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#6-csrf-with-cookiecsrftokenrepository-and-angulars-xsrf-support-csrfspa-in-spring-security-7))
- **`CookieCsrfTokenRepository`**: Spring Security's store for the expected CSRF token in an `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie, read back from `X-XSRF-TOKEN` ([42 §6](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#6-csrf-with-cookiecsrftokenrepository-and-angulars-xsrf-support-csrfspa-in-spring-security-7))
- **BREACH**: an attack on HTTPS through HTTP compression; Spring Security defends its CSRF token by masking it ([42 §6](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#6-csrf-with-cookiecsrftokenrepository-and-angulars-xsrf-support-csrfspa-in-spring-security-7))
- **masked token**: a CSRF token XORed with fresh random bytes and sent together with them, so it differs on every response ([42 §6](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#6-csrf-with-cookiecsrftokenrepository-and-angulars-xsrf-support-csrfspa-in-spring-security-7))
- **deferred token**: a CSRF token that Spring Security loads only when a request needs it ([42 §6](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#6-csrf-with-cookiecsrftokenrepository-and-angulars-xsrf-support-csrfspa-in-spring-security-7))
- **`csrf.spa()`**: Spring Security 7's CSRF setting for single-page apps: a script-readable cookie and a resolver that accepts the raw header value ([42 §6](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#6-csrf-with-cookiecsrftokenrepository-and-angulars-xsrf-support-csrfspa-in-spring-security-7))
- **login CSRF**: a forged sign-in that logs the victim into the attacker's account ([42 §6](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#6-csrf-with-cookiecsrftokenrepository-and-angulars-xsrf-support-csrfspa-in-spring-security-7))
- **auth state**: what the page knows about the visitor: unknown, anonymous or signed in; session state loaded once at startup ([42 §7](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#7-the-angular-side-an-auth-state-at-startup-guards-as-ux-interceptors-and-angular-oauth2-oidc-versus-angular-auth-oidc-client))
- **app initializer**: a function that the application awaits before it starts, registered with `provideAppInitializer` ([42 §7](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#7-the-angular-side-an-auth-state-at-startup-guards-as-ux-interceptors-and-angular-oauth2-oidc-versus-angular-auth-oidc-client))
- **`canMatch` guard**: a route guard that decides whether the router considers a route at all ([42 §7](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#7-the-angular-side-an-auth-state-at-startup-guards-as-ux-interceptors-and-angular-oauth2-oidc-versus-angular-auth-oidc-client))
- **return URL**: the path a visitor asked for before being sent to sign in, carried so the app can take them back after ([42 §7](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#7-the-angular-side-an-auth-state-at-startup-guards-as-ux-interceptors-and-angular-oauth2-oidc-versus-angular-auth-oidc-client))
- **class interceptor**: an `HttpInterceptor` class registered under `HTTP_INTERCEPTORS`, the legacy form that runs only with `withInterceptorsFromDi()` ([42 §7](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#7-the-angular-side-an-auth-state-at-startup-guards-as-ux-interceptors-and-angular-oauth2-oidc-versus-angular-auth-oidc-client))
- **OIDC client library**: a package that runs the OAuth or OpenID Connect flow inside the page, such as `angular-oauth2-oidc` or `angular-auth-oidc-client` ([42 §7](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#7-the-angular-side-an-auth-state-at-startup-guards-as-ux-interceptors-and-angular-oauth2-oidc-versus-angular-auth-oidc-client))
- **open redirect**: a redirect that follows an untrusted URL to another site, so an attacker's link sends the visitor to the attacker's page ([42 §7](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#7-the-angular-side-an-auth-state-at-startup-guards-as-ux-interceptors-and-angular-oauth2-oidc-versus-angular-auth-oidc-client))
- **local logout**: ending only the app's own session and client state, which leaves the provider's session and other devices alone ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **RP-initiated logout**: the app (the relying party) sending the browser to the identity provider's logout URL so the provider ends its session too ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **end-session endpoint**: the identity provider's logout URL, published as `end_session_endpoint` in its discovery metadata ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **back-channel logout**: the identity provider telling each app, server to server, to end a user's sessions, with a logout token ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **logout token**: the signed JWT of back-channel logout; it names a user (`sub`) or a provider session (`sid`) and has an `events` claim ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **token revocation**: asking the authorization server to invalidate one token (RFC 7009) ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **`Clear-Site-Data`**: a response header that tells the browser to clear an origin's cookies, storage or cache ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **idle timeout**: the time without requests after which a server ends a session ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **`BroadcastChannel`**: a browser API that sends messages between same-origin tabs and frames by channel name ([42 §8](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#8-logout-everywhere-and-session-timeout-ux))
- **trace table (request path)**: a list of one login's hops, each with what it reads and what it writes ([42 §9](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#9-under-the-hood-one-bff-login-and-the-first-api-call-through-spring-securitys-filters))
- **filter order**: each Spring Security filter's fixed place in the chain, set by the internal `FilterOrderRegistration` ([42 §9](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#9-under-the-hood-one-bff-login-and-the-first-api-call-through-spring-securitys-filters))
- **saved request**: a request Spring Security keeps in the session to return to after login ([42 §9](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#9-under-the-hood-one-bff-login-and-the-first-api-call-through-spring-securitys-filters))
- **session fixation**: an attack that plants a session id before the victim signs in ([42 §9](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#9-under-the-hood-one-bff-login-and-the-first-api-call-through-spring-securitys-filters))
- **login entry**: the one route every signed-out path leads to, here our `/login` route; its button starts the full-page BFF login ([Exercise 42.3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#ex42-3))
- **tab channel**: our `TabChannel` interface over `BroadcastChannel`, with `post` and `onMessage`, so tests can fake the other tabs ([Exercise 42.3](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#ex42-3))
- **userinfo endpoint**: the OpenID Connect endpoint that returns claims about the signed-in user when called with an access token; it is an OAuth 2.0 protected resource ([Q42.06](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#q42-06))

## Module 43

- **polling**: the client asks the server again every N seconds, and nothing stays open between asks ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **long polling**: the server holds a request until there is news or a timeout, and the client asks again at once (RFC 6202) ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **Server-Sent Events (SSE)**: one HTTP response the server keeps open and writes text events into, one way ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **WebSocket**: one HTTP request upgraded into a two-way channel of frames, with no reconnection or message format built in (RFC 6455) ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **full duplex**: both sides of a connection may send at any time ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **Web Push**: delivering a message to a browser with no tab open, through the browser vendor's push service (RFC 8030) ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **push service**: the browser vendor's server that receives an application server's push request and delivers it to the browser ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **VAPID**: Voluntary Application Server Identification, a signed ES256 JWT in an `Authorization: vapid` header that tells the push service who sends (RFC 8292) ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **`TTL` header**: the push message's lifetime in seconds, which a push service requires and may shorten (RFC 8030 §5.2) ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **per-host connection limit**: the number of connections a browser opens to one server, 6 or 8 under HTTP/1.1, which each open stream or held request uses one of (RFC 6202 §5.1) ([43 §1](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#1-choosing-a-transport-polling-long-polling-server-sent-events-websockets-and-web-push-when-no-tab-is-open))
- **event stream**: the `text/event-stream` body of an SSE response, UTF-8 text in `name: value` lines grouped into events by blank lines (WHATWG HTML §9.2.5) ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **field (event stream)**: one `name: value` line of an event stream; `data`, `event`, `id` and `retry` are known, others are ignored ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **dispatch (SSE)**: what a blank line does: the event built so far fires, unless its data buffer is empty ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **data buffer**: the text an event's `data` lines build, each value followed by an LF; the final LF is removed at dispatch ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **event type**: the name an `event` field gives an event, `message` when none is given ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **last event ID**: the value of the latest `id` field, which persists across events and is copied onto each event's `lastEventId` ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **`Last-Event-ID`**: the request header an `EventSource` sends on reconnecting when its last event ID is not empty ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **reconnection time**: how long an `EventSource` waits before reconnecting, in milliseconds, set by a `retry` field of ASCII digits only ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **comment line**: an event-stream line starting with `:`, which the parser ignores ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **heartbeat**: a message sent only to keep an idle connection alive or detect a dead one; for SSE, a comment line every 15 seconds or so ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **`readyState`**: an `EventSource`'s state, `CONNECTING`, `OPEN` or `CLOSED`; after `error` it tells a retry (`CONNECTING`) from a failure (`CLOSED`) ([43 §2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#2-server-sent-events-on-the-wire-the-textevent-stream-format-and-how-eventsource-reconnects))
- **`SseEmitter`**: Spring MVC's return value for streaming events: the controller returns it, and any thread later writes events into the open response ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **async request processing**: a Servlet request put in asynchronous mode so the Servlet thread can return while the response stays open for later writes (Spring MVC reference, Asynchronous Requests) ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **emitter registry**: the map of live emitters that sending code walks, and that callbacks must remove emitters from ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **async request timeout**: how long an async request may stay open: the emitter's own value, else MVC's default (`spring.mvc.async.request-timeout`), else the container's ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **WebFlux**: Spring's reactive, non-blocking web stack, where a controller can return a `Flux` of events ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **`ServerSentEvent`**: WebFlux's type for one event, with builder methods for `id`, `event`, `retry`, `comment` and `data` ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **event log (replay buffer)**: a bounded list of recent events with ids, from which a reconnecting client's missed events are replayed, or a reload is requested when they are gone ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **heartbeat comment**: a comment-only event sent periodically so that the write fails when the client has gone ([43 §3](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#3-server-sent-events-in-spring-sseemitter-in-spring-mvc-and-a-flux-of-serversentevent-in-webflux))
- **`withCredentials`**: the only option of `new EventSource(url, { withCredentials })`; it sets the credentials mode to `include` so cross-origin requests carry cookies, and no header can be set ([43 §4](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#4-the-angular-sse-client-eventsource-or-a-fetch-stream-as-an-observable-and-when-httpclient-fits))
- **named event**: an event whose stream block has an `event:` field; only a listener added for that name receives it, not `onmessage` ([43 §4](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#4-the-angular-sse-client-eventsource-or-a-fetch-stream-as-an-observable-and-when-httpclient-fits))
- **`AbortController`**: the platform object whose `signal` cancels a `fetch`; calling `abort()` ends the request and its body stream ([43 §4](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#4-the-angular-sse-client-eventsource-or-a-fetch-stream-as-an-observable-and-when-httpclient-fits))
- **`TextDecoderStream`**: a platform stream that turns chunks of bytes into chunks of text, keeping a character split across chunks whole ([43 §4](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#4-the-angular-sse-client-eventsource-or-a-fetch-stream-as-an-observable-and-when-httpclient-fits))
- **download progress event**: `HttpClient`'s event for a response body in progress; its `partialText` is the cumulative text so far ([43 §4](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#4-the-angular-sse-client-eventsource-or-a-fetch-stream-as-an-observable-and-when-httpclient-fits))
- **opening handshake**: the HTTP `GET` with `Upgrade: websocket` that turns a connection into a WebSocket (RFC 6455 §1.3) ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **`101 Switching Protocols`**: the server's answer that accepts the upgrade; from then on the connection speaks WebSocket ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **frame (WebSocket)**: the unit of data on a WebSocket connection, sent by either side after the handshake ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **masking**: XOR of a frame's payload with a random key; a client masks every frame it sends, a server none (RFC 6455 §5.1) ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **close code**: the status number a WebSocket Close frame carries, 1000 for a normal closure (RFC 6455 §7.4.1) ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **STOMP**: Simple Text Oriented Messaging Protocol; frames of a command, headers, a blank line, a body and a NULL octet, carried here inside WebSocket messages (STOMP 1.2) ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **destination (STOMP)**: the header that says where a `SEND` goes or what a `SUBSCRIBE` listens to, such as `/app/room.1` or `/topic/room.1` ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **application destination prefix**: the prefix (`/app`) that routes a message to `@MessageMapping` methods, removed before matching ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **`@MessageMapping`**: Spring's annotation for a method that handles messages whose destination matches its pattern, as `@RequestMapping` does for paths ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **simple broker**: Spring's in-memory STOMP broker: it stores subscriptions and broadcasts, with no acks or receipts and no second instance ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **user destination**: a `/user/…` destination that Spring resolves to one destination per session of a user ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **`@SendToUser`**: sends a message method's return value to the user's sessions (or only the sending one with `broadcast = false`) instead of every subscriber ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **heart-beat (STOMP)**: an EOL sent when no frame is due; `heart-beat:<can send>,<wants>` on `CONNECT` and `CONNECTED` sets each direction to the max of the two, or none when either is 0 ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **SockJS**: a fallback transport for networks or browsers without WebSocket, enabled in Spring with `withSockJS()`; legacy today ([43 §5](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#5-websockets-and-stomp-in-spring-the-handshake-frames-messagemapping-brokers-user-destinations-and-heart-beats))
- **`webSocket` / `WebSocketSubject`**: RxJS's Subject over one browser socket: `next` sends, subscribers receive, a clean close completes it and any other close or an `error` event errors it ([43 §6](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#6-the-angular-websocket-client-rxjs-websocket-stomprx-stomp-and-reconnecting-without-losing-subscriptions))
- **clean close (`wasClean`)**: a close after the closing handshake finished (RFC 6455 §7.1.4); RxJS completes the stream on it and errors otherwise ([43 §6](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#6-the-angular-websocket-client-rxjs-websocket-stomprx-stomp-and-reconnecting-without-losing-subscriptions))
- **resubscription**: resending on every open what the server must know, such as a subscribe request per topic, because a new socket remembers nothing ([43 §6](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#6-the-angular-websocket-client-rxjs-websocket-stomprx-stomp-and-reconnecting-without-losing-subscriptions))
- **`@stomp/rx-stomp`**: an RxJS wrapper over stompjs whose `watch` re-subscribes after each reconnect and whose `publish` queues while offline; read, not run ([43 §6](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#6-the-angular-websocket-client-rxjs-websocket-stomprx-stomp-and-reconnecting-without-losing-subscriptions))
- **handshake principal**: the user authenticated on the HTTP upgrade request, which Spring keeps for the WebSocket session and stamps on every STOMP message ([43 §7](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#7-authentication-on-the-handshake-cookies-tokens-in-the-connect-frame-origin-checks-and-csrf-in-spring-security))
- **`ChannelInterceptor`**: Spring Messaging's hook on a message channel; its `preSend` sees each inbound message and may change it (set the user) or throw to refuse it ([43 §7](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#7-authentication-on-the-handshake-cookies-tokens-in-the-connect-frame-origin-checks-and-csrf-in-spring-security))
- **cross-site WebSocket hijacking**: another site's page opening a WebSocket to your server that carries the user's cookies, possible because browsers apply no same-origin policy to WebSocket ([43 §7](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#7-authentication-on-the-handshake-cookies-tokens-in-the-connect-frame-origin-checks-and-csrf-in-spring-security))
- **origin check**: the server comparing the handshake's `Origin` header with its own origin or an allowed list, answering 403 otherwise (RFC 6455 §10.2); it guards browsers only ([43 §7](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#7-authentication-on-the-handshake-cookies-tokens-in-the-connect-frame-origin-checks-and-csrf-in-spring-security))
- **CSRF token on `CONNECT`**: the token Spring Security requires in a `CONNECT` frame header by default, compared with the one copied from the handshake request; it must be the masked value ([43 §7](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#7-authentication-on-the-handshake-cookies-tokens-in-the-connect-frame-origin-checks-and-csrf-in-spring-security))
- **message authorization**: rules by message type and destination that allow or deny each inbound STOMP message; the first matching rule decides and no match denies ([43 §7](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#7-authentication-on-the-handshake-cookies-tokens-in-the-connect-frame-origin-checks-and-csrf-in-spring-security))
- **`@EnableWebSocketSecurity`**: Spring Security's annotation (`@since 5.8`) that secures the inbound channel with a security-context, a CSRF and an authorization interceptor ([43 §7](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#7-authentication-on-the-handshake-cookies-tokens-in-the-connect-frame-origin-checks-and-csrf-in-spring-security))
- **connection service**: the one service that owns the socket and shares it, as one Observable and a state signal, with every reader ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **connection-state signal**: a signal holding `connecting`, `open`, `reconnecting` or `closed`, for a banner that says whether the data is live ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **grace period**: the wait after the last reader leaves during which a returning reader cancels the close, so a route change keeps the socket ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **snapshot (state)**: the whole current state, loaded over HTTP, which later deltas are applied to ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **delta**: one change after a snapshot, sent over the live connection with a sequence number ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **sequence number**: the server's counter on each delta, which lets the client ignore what the snapshot holds and notice what is missing ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **gap**: a delta whose sequence number skips the next expected one, meaning a change was lost, so the store reloads the snapshot ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **conflation**: merging a burst of updates into one write per window, so the array copy and the signal write run once per window ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **`rxResource` with `stream`**: a resource whose loader returns an Observable; each value replaces the resource's value, and completing with no value is an error (NG0991) ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **streaming `resource`**: `resource({ stream })`, whose loader returns a signal of `{ value }` or `{ error }` items that can change over time ([43 §8](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#8-from-streams-to-signals-one-shared-connection-tosignal-and-rxresource-bursts-and-connection-state))
- **broker relay**: Spring's handler that forwards `/topic` and `/queue` messages to an external STOMP broker such as RabbitMQ, over one TCP connection per client session plus a system connection ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **system connection**: the relay's one shared TCP connection to the broker, used for messages from application code (no session id) ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **fan-out**: copying one message to every subscriber of a destination; a simple broker does it only inside its own JVM ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **`BrokerAvailabilityEvent`**: Spring's application event saying the relay's system connection was lost or re-established ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **user registry broadcast**: a destination on which each instance shares its user registry every 10 s, so another instance can resolve a user's sessions ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **send buffer**: the per-session queue where Spring keeps outgoing messages while another send to that client is in progress ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **send time limit**: how long one send to a client may take (10 s by default) before Spring closes the session ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **overflow strategy**: what Spring does when a send buffer passes its limit: `TERMINATE` (close with 4500, the default) or `DROP` (discard the oldest messages) ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **sticky session**: a load balancer sending one client to the same instance every time ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances), [42 §1](../modules/42-fullstack-authentication.md#1-sessions-versus-tokens-what-the-server-keeps-what-each-request-carries-revocation-and-scaling))
- **reconnect storm**: many clients reconnecting at the same moment, for example after a deploy closes every socket on an instance ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **jitter**: a random part added to each reconnection wait so that clients spread out instead of returning together ([43 §9](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#9-scaling-out-and-under-the-hood-sticky-sessions-the-broker-relay-backpressure-and-one-message-traced-across-two-instances))
- **watchdog (timer)**: a timer that every received frame restarts; if it fires, the connection has been silent too long and is treated as dead ([Exercise 43.2](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#ex43-2))
- **presence**: which users are connected on which instance; each instance knows only its own until the user registry broadcast shares it ([Q43.19](../modules/43-fullstack-realtime.md#q43-19))

## Module 44

- **direct call**: the browser calling a service's own origin, with no server in between, so it is cross-origin unless the host matches ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **reverse proxy**: a server that forwards a client's requests to other servers and returns their answers ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **same-origin deployment**: serving the app and its API from one scheme, host and port, so the browser sends no cross-origin request ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **API gateway**: one entry point for many services that applies cross-cutting concerns such as routing, TLS and rate limits ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **backend-for-frontend (BFF)**: a backend built for one front end that aggregates service calls and holds the session ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **aggregation**: merging several service calls into one answer, so the browser pays one round trip ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **edge**: the entry point of a system, where requests first arrive ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **hop (network)**: one more network step between the browser and the service that answers ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **cross-cutting concern**: work every request needs whatever the service, such as routing, TLS, rate limiting or request IDs ([44 §1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#1-where-the-api-calls-go-direct-calls-a-same-origin-reverse-proxy-an-api-gateway-or-a-backend-for-frontend))
- **image**: a read-only stack of layers, built from a base image, that a container runs ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **container**: a running instance of an image ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **layer**: one read-only filesystem change an image keeps, made by one Dockerfile instruction ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **base image**: the image a Dockerfile starts from with `FROM` ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **build context**: the folder sent to the builder, which is all a `COPY` can read ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **`.dockerignore`**: a file listing paths removed from the build context before it is sent to the builder ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **multi-stage build**: a Dockerfile with several `FROM` lines, where a later stage copies chosen files from an earlier one and leaves the rest behind ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **build stage**: the stage that installs dependencies and builds the app, and is not part of the final image ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **runtime stage**: the last stage, whose result is the image that runs ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **layer cache**: the stored layers Docker reuses while an instruction and its inputs are unchanged; a changed layer invalidates every later one ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **cache mount**: a folder, such as npm's download cache, that a `RUN --mount=type=cache` step keeps between builds ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **unprivileged image**: the `nginx-unprivileged` image, which runs Nginx as a non-root user (UID 101) on port 8080 ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **layered jar**: a Spring Boot jar split into layers, such as dependencies and application code, so Docker caches the stable ones ([44 §2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#2-a-docker-multi-stage-build-for-angular-build-and-runtime-stages-layer-caching-dockerignore-and-a-non-root-image))
- **`location` (Nginx)**: an Nginx block that configures the requests whose URI it matches ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **exact match (Nginx)**: a `location =` block, used only for exactly that URI; finding one ends the search ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **prefix location**: a `location` given as a plain string, matching URIs that start with it; the longest match is remembered ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **regex location**: a `location ~` (case-sensitive) or `location ~*` (case-insensitive) block, matched by a regular expression ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **normalized URI**: the request URI after decoding `%XX`, resolving `.` and `..`, and merging repeated slashes; what Nginx matches locations against ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`try_files`**: an Nginx directive that uses the first listed file that exists, else a status (`=404`) or an internal redirect ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **internal redirect**: Nginx restarting location matching with a new URI inside the server, without telling the browser ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **SPA fallback**: `try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html`: an unknown path gets `index.html`, so the client-side router handles it ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`add_header` level**: one configuration level (`http`, `server`, `location`) that inherits the parent's `add_header` fields only if it declares none ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`always`**: the `add_header` parameter that adds the field whatever the status, error responses included ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`add_header_inherit`**: an Nginx directive (1.29.3+) whose `merge` value appends the parent level's `add_header` fields to the current level's ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`gzip_types`**: the MIME types Nginx gzips in addition to `text/html`, its default ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`Vary: Accept-Encoding`**: a response header telling caches to keep a copy per `Accept-Encoding` value, owned by [09 §4](../modules/09-web-networking-storage-security.md#4-http-caching) ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **precompressed file**: a compressed copy, such as `main.js.gz`, written before deployment and sent instead of the original ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`gzip_static`**: an Nginx module, not built by default, that sends `file.gz` instead of `file` when it exists and the client accepts gzip ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **Brotli**: a compression format denser than gzip's deflate ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **`ngx_brotli`**: Google's third-party pair of Nginx modules (on-the-fly and static) that adds Brotli; nginx open source has none ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **predicate location**: a `location $variable` block (mainline 1.31.5) that matches when the variable is non-empty and not "0", checked after regex locations ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **Baseline**: MDN's label for browser support: newly available in all current major browsers, or widely available for 2.5+ years ([44 §3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#3-nginx-for-a-single-page-app-location-matching-the-try_files-fallback-caching-headers-for-hashed-assets-and-gzip-or-brotli))
- **upstream**: the server a reverse proxy forwards requests to, here Spring Boot ([44 §4](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#4-nginx-as-the-reverse-proxy-proxy_pass-forwarded-headers-into-spring-boot-and-sse-or-websocket-connections-through-the-proxy))
- **hop-by-hop header**: a header meant for one connection only, so a proxy does not pass it on; `Upgrade` and `Connection` are examples ([44 §4](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#4-nginx-as-the-reverse-proxy-proxy_pass-forwarded-headers-into-spring-boot-and-sse-or-websocket-connections-through-the-proxy))
- **proxy buffering**: Nginx collecting a proxied response in buffers before sending it to the client; `proxy_buffering off` sends data as it arrives ([44 §4](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#4-nginx-as-the-reverse-proxy-proxy_pass-forwarded-headers-into-spring-boot-and-sse-or-websocket-connections-through-the-proxy))
- **TLS termination**: ending the HTTPS connection at the proxy, so the server behind it receives plain HTTP and must be told the original scheme ([44 §4](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#4-nginx-as-the-reverse-proxy-proxy_pass-forwarded-headers-into-spring-boot-and-sse-or-websocket-connections-through-the-proxy))
- **`proxy_read_timeout`**: the Nginx timeout (60 seconds by default) between two successive reads from the proxied server; a longer silence closes the connection ([44 §4](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#4-nginx-as-the-reverse-proxy-proxy_pass-forwarded-headers-into-spring-boot-and-sse-or-websocket-connections-through-the-proxy))
- **entrypoint script**: a shell file the container runs before its main process; in the nginx image, an executable `*.sh` file in `/docker-entrypoint.d` ([44 §5](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#5-runtime-configuration-without-rebuilding-configjson-environment-substitution-at-container-start-and-provideappinitializer))
- **`/docker-entrypoint.d`**: the folder whose executable `*.sh` files the nginx image's `docker-entrypoint.sh` runs, in `sort -V` order, before it starts Nginx ([44 §5](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#5-runtime-configuration-without-rebuilding-configjson-environment-substitution-at-container-start-and-provideappinitializer))
- **config template**: a file with `${NAME}` placeholders that a start-up step fills from the environment to produce the real file, such as `config.json` ([44 §5](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#5-runtime-configuration-without-rebuilding-configjson-environment-substitution-at-container-start-and-provideappinitializer))
- **`envsubst`**: GNU gettext's program that replaces `$NAME` and `${NAME}` in text with environment values, only for the listed names when a list is given ([44 §5](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#5-runtime-configuration-without-rebuilding-configjson-environment-substitution-at-container-start-and-provideappinitializer))
- **variable filter**: `NGINX_ENVSUBST_FILTER`, a pattern that limits which defined environment variables the nginx image's template step lists for `envsubst` ([44 §5](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#5-runtime-configuration-without-rebuilding-configjson-environment-substitution-at-container-start-and-provideappinitializer))
- **required variable**: an environment variable whose absence stops the start instead of producing a broken file ([44 §5](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#5-runtime-configuration-without-rebuilding-configjson-environment-substitution-at-container-start-and-provideappinitializer))
- **fail fast**: stopping at the first sign of misconfiguration, so the orchestrator sees the failure, instead of running on with a bad value ([44 §5](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#5-runtime-configuration-without-rebuilding-configjson-environment-substitution-at-container-start-and-provideappinitializer))
- **signal (process)**: a notice the kernel delivers to a process, such as SIGTERM or SIGKILL; a process may handle, ignore or take the default action ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **SIGTERM**: the signal that asks a process to stop; a server can catch it and shut down gracefully ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **SIGKILL**: the signal that ends a process at once; it cannot be caught, so nothing drains ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **stop signal**: the signal `docker stop` sends first, SIGTERM unless `STOPSIGNAL` or `--stop-signal` sets another ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **grace period (shutdown)**: how long `docker stop` (10 s by default for Linux containers) or an orchestrator waits after the stop signal before sending SIGKILL ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **PID 1**: a container's first process; Linux ignores signals with the default action for it, so it stops only if it handles them ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **exec form**: a Dockerfile `CMD` or `ENTRYPOINT` written as a JSON array, run directly without a shell ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **shell form**: a Dockerfile `CMD` or `ENTRYPOINT` written as a plain string, run under `/bin/sh -c`, which does not pass signals on ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **init process**: a small PID 1, such as tini behind `docker run --init`, that forwards signals to the application and reaps finished processes ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **graceful shutdown**: stopping in order: mark not ready, let routing stop, stop accepting, finish in-flight requests, exit before the deadline ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **drain**: letting the requests already in flight finish after new connections stop being accepted ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **liveness probe**: a check whose failure makes the orchestrator restart the container; it must not depend on rendering or other services ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **readiness probe**: a check whose failure takes the instance out of the load balancer without restarting it ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **`HEALTHCHECK`**: the Dockerfile instruction that runs a command periodically and marks the container unhealthy after `--retries` consecutive failures ([44 §6](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#6-serving-ssr-apps-in-containers-the-node-server-as-pid-1-graceful-shutdown-health-probes-and-what-nginx-or-a-cdn-still-serves))
- **continuous delivery**: keeping every change that passes the pipeline ready to deploy, by extending the pipeline's stages to the deployment itself ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **image tag**: a label on a container image that the registry can move to another image ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **image digest**: the SHA-256 of a container image's content; it names that exact image and cannot be moved to another ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **promotion**: deploying the image digest that passed one stage to the next stage, without rebuilding it ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **path filter**: a rule that runs a pipeline only when a changed file lies under a watched folder ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **contract gate**: the pipeline step that diffs the committed API description against the previous one and fails the pull request on a breaking change ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **expand and contract**: shipping a breaking change in three steps: an API that accepts old and new, then the client, then the removal of the old form after the support window ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **support window**: how long the old contract must keep working, bounded by how long old tabs and cached bundles may still run ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **compatibility matrix**: a table of which client versions work with which API releases, needed when the two layers live in separate repositories ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **polyrepo**: a setup in which the front end and the back end live in separate repositories and share the contract by publishing it ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract))
- **feature flag**: a switch that chooses a code path at run time, so a change can be turned on or off without a deploy ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **OpenFeature**: a vendor-neutral API and SDKs for evaluating flags; the provider, not OpenFeature, does the evaluation ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **client (OpenFeature)**: the object the application asks for a typed flag value with a default ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **provider (OpenFeature)**: the vendor's or your own code that resolves a flag value from the evaluation context ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **evaluation context**: the data a flag evaluation may use, such as the targeting key and attributes of the user ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **targeting key**: the string that identifies the subject of an evaluation, usually the user ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **static-context paradigm**: client-side evaluation for one user, with the context set globally and asynchronously and evaluations synchronous afterwards ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **dynamic-context paradigm**: server-side evaluation on behalf of many users, with a context per request ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **variant**: a named value a flag can resolve to ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **resolution reason**: the field that says why an evaluation returned its value, such as `SPLIT`, `DISABLED` or `ERROR` ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **percentage rollout**: giving a share of users one variant and the rest another ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **bucket**: the slot a hash of the flag key and targeting key puts a user in; a rollout serves the new variant to the lowest slots ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **sticky assignment**: a user keeps the same variant (or release) on every evaluation because it depends only on stable inputs ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
