- **kill switch (flag sense)**: turning a flag off at the provider so the feature stops without a deploy; not the service worker's kill switch of module 37 ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **canary release**: sending a slice of traffic to a new version before the rest ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **blue-green deployment**: running two full environments and flipping all traffic from one to the other ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **version skew**: one tab holding files from two releases, such as `index.html` from one and a chunk from another ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew))
- **release identifier**: one string that names a build, used by the build, the tracker and every report so maps and events match ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **source-map upload**: sending the build's source maps to the error tracker under a release identifier ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **debug ID**: an ID inserted into a minified file and its source map so a tracker can match them without a public URL ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **RUM collector**: the endpoint that receives real-user-monitoring events ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **page-view id**: an identifier for one document load, stamped on every event of that page view ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **per-page-view sampling**: drawing once, when the page view starts, whether it reports, so a sampled page view sends every event and any other none ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **beacon**: a small asynchronous HTTP POST (`navigator.sendBeacon`) the browser sends without delaying the page's exit ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **`visibilitychange`**: the event that fires when a tab becomes hidden or visible; MDN calls it the most reliable moment to send last data ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **bfcache**: the back/forward cache, which restores a whole page on Back; an `unload` listener makes a page ineligible ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **error rate**: errors per 1,000 page views, compared per release instead of counting errors ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling))
- **distributed trace**: the tree of spans that share one trace ID across browser, proxy and server ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **span**: one timed operation in a trace, with its own span ID and the span ID of its caller ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **trace ID**: the 32-hex-digit identifier that every span of one trace carries; all zeroes is invalid (W3C Trace Context §3.2.2.3) ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **parent ID** (span ID): the 16-hex-digit identifier of the calling span, sent as the third field of `traceparent` ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **trace flags**: the last two hex digits of `traceparent`; only the sampled bit is defined in version 00 ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **sampled flag**: trace-flags bit 1 (`01`), meaning the caller may have recorded trace data; a recommendation, not a rule ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **`traceparent`**: the W3C Trace Context header `00-<trace-id>-<parent-id>-<trace-flags>` that carries a trace across hops ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **`tracestate`**: the W3C Trace Context companion header for vendor-specific trace data ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **propagation**: sending the trace context to the next hop in a header so its spans join the same trace ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **context manager**: the OpenTelemetry component that holds the active span for code running now ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **`StackContextManager`**: the browser SDK's default context manager; the context is active only during a synchronous call, so it is lost after `await` ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **`ZoneContextManager`**: OpenTelemetry's zone.js-based context manager (`@opentelemetry/context-zone`), which follows async work but needs zone.js ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **head sampling**: deciding whether a trace is recorded when it starts, before anything about its outcome is known ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **MDC (logging)**: the logging framework's per-thread context map, from which Spring Boot builds the `[traceId-spanId]` correlation ID ([44 §10](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#10-under-the-hood-opentelemetry-for-the-web-traceparent-and-one-click-traced-to-a-spring-boot-span-and-log-line))
- **release manifest**: in Exercise 44.1, the `releases.json` object listing each kept release with its hashed keys, written before `index.html` so a later publish knows which old chunks it may delete ([Exercise 44.1](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#ex44-1))
- **interaction**: in Exercise 44.2, one user action, such as a click, whose requests share one trace ID and one sampling decision ([Exercise 44.2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#ex44-2))
- **backend decorator**: in Exercise 44.2, a class that implements `HttpBackend`, wraps the real backend and is provided in its place, so it sees each request after every interceptor ([Exercise 44.2](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#ex44-2))
- **`RECONCILING`**: in `@openfeature/web-sdk` 1.10.0, the provider state while flags for a new evaluation context are still loading, entered when the provider's `onContextChange` returns a promise ([Exercise 44.3](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#ex44-3))
- **correlation ID**: an ID such as `X-Request-ID` that names one request across logs, not the tree of spans a trace ID joins ([Q44.19](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#q44-19))

## Module 45

- **system design round**: an open question about a whole product, answered by talking and drawing rather than by finishing code ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **machine-coding round**: a round in which you build one component live ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **functional requirement**: a statement of what the product does ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **non-functional requirement**: a statement of how well the product does it, such as speed, accessibility or availability ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **the method**: this module's eight steps: requirements, components, data and flow, state, API, performance, accessibility, failure modes ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **running example**: the team task board that carries every step of the method ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **failure mode**: a way a design breaks ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **observability**: how you would notice a failure mode, through signals such as errors and traces ([45 §1](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#1-the-front-end-system-design-interview-what-it-evaluates-its-formats-and-how-it-differs-from-a-backend-design-round))
- **time plan**: how the slot's minutes are split across the method's steps ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **checkpoint (interview)**: a pause where you ask the interviewer where to go deeper ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **deep dive**: one step explored far past its planned share ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **trade-off statement**: a decision stated with its axis, options, deciding condition, accepted cost and revisit trigger ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **zoom level**: one of the three diagram levels used here, after C4: system context, containers, components ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **system context**: the diagram level showing the user, our app and the systems around it ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **C4 container**: a runtime boundary around code that runs or data that is stored; not an Angular container component ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **component diagram**: the diagram level that opens one container into its parts ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **labeled arrow**: an arrow that says what flows and in which direction ([45 §2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#2-communicating-under-time-pressure-a-time-plan-complete-trade-off-statements-and-diagrams-at-three-zoom-levels))
- **trust boundary**: where data reaches a more trusted side ([33 §1](../modules/33-security.md#1-threat-model-of-an-angular-single-page-app-what-the-attacker-controls-what-runs-where-and-where-each-defense-lives))
- **constraint (design)**: a limit fixed from outside the design, such as team, existing API or deadline ([45 §3](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#3-requirements-first-functional-and-non-functional-requirements-constraints-and-back-of-envelope-numbers))
- **back-of-envelope estimate**: arithmetic good to an order of magnitude ([45 §3](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#3-requirements-first-functional-and-non-functional-requirements-constraints-and-back-of-envelope-numbers))
- **network profile**: a named round-trip time and throughput used to estimate or simulate loading ([45 §3](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#3-requirements-first-functional-and-non-functional-requirements-constraints-and-back-of-envelope-numbers))
- **round trip**: one request and its response; RTT is its duration ([45 §3](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#3-requirements-first-functional-and-non-functional-requirements-constraints-and-back-of-envelope-numbers))
- **throughput**: bytes moved per unit of time ([45 §3](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#3-requirements-first-functional-and-non-functional-requirements-constraints-and-back-of-envelope-numbers))
- **simulated throttling**: computing a load's timing from a recorded trace instead of slowing the real network ([45 §3](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#3-requirements-first-functional-and-non-functional-requirements-constraints-and-back-of-envelope-numbers))
- **component tree**: a diagram of which component renders which ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **container (component)**: a component that reads state and passes it down; a presentational component takes inputs and emits outputs ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **presentational component**: a component that takes inputs and emits outputs and fetches nothing ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **feature boundary**: the limit around one feature's files ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **lazy boundary**: a route or block loaded on demand ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **entity**: a thing with an id, such as a board, column, card, comment or user ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **client-side model**: how the browser keeps entities in memory ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **nested shape**: entities stored inside their parents, as an API returns them ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **normalized shape**: entities stored once in id-keyed maps and referred to by id ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **unidirectional data flow**: data goes down through inputs and events go up through outputs ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **request waterfall**: requests that start only after earlier ones finish ([45 §4](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#4-component-architecture-data-model-and-data-flow-from-screens-to-a-component-tree-and-a-client-side-model))
- **state placement**: choosing the home of each piece of state ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **source of truth**: the one place whose value wins when copies disagree ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **shareable state**: state a shared link or the back button must restore ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **ephemeral state**: state that dies with the interaction that made it ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **resource (REST)**: a thing the API names by URL ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **first-screen read**: the data the first view needs to render ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **aggregated endpoint**: an endpoint that returns several resources in one response ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **cursor**: a marker for the position after the last item of a page ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **live update**: a change the server pushes to the client ([45 §5](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#5-state-and-api-design-where-each-piece-of-state-lives-and-the-api-the-screens-need))
- **performance budget**: a limit on what a screen may cost, in bytes, requests or time ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **loading plan**: what a route loads up front and what waits ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **windowing**: rendering only the rows that are visible ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **skeleton**: a placeholder that reserves a loading area's size ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **single-pointer alternative**: a way to operate a drag with clicks or taps instead ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **target size**: the clickable area of a control ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **focus restoration**: returning focus to a sensible element after an action ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **announcement**: text a screen reader speaks, usually through a live region ([45 §6](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#6-performance-and-accessibility-as-design-steps-budgets-a-loading-plan-long-lists-and-keyboard-and-pointer-paths))
- **rendering strategy**: where and when a route's HTML is built ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **prerendering**: building HTML at build time ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **caching layer**: a store that answers requests under a key ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **cache key**: the identity under which an answer is stored ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **cache hit**: a layer answers from a stored entry ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **cache miss**: a layer has no usable entry and asks the next ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **staleness**: how old an answer may be ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **invalidation**: removing or refreshing a stored entry ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **shared cache**: a cache that serves many users, such as a CDN ([45 §7](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#7-rendering-strategy-and-caching-layers-a-rendering-choice-per-route-and-every-cache-from-the-cdn-to-the-component))
- **degraded behavior**: what a screen does while a dependency fails ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **signal (telemetry)**: the data that reports a failure, such as an error report, a trace or a count ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **offline level**: how much of the app works without a network: none, cached reads or queued writes ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **outbox**: a client-side queue of writes waiting to be sent ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **operation id**: an id on each queued write so the server applies a resent write once ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **replay**: sending the queued writes, in order, after reconnecting ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **last-write-wins**: a write policy that keeps the latest save and silently drops earlier concurrent ones ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **conditional write**: a write that carries a precondition, such as `If-Match` with the entity tag the client read ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **precondition**: a request header condition the server tests before performing the method (RFC 9110 §13) ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **field-level merge**: a write policy that accepts concurrent edits to different fields and refuses edits to a field changed since the read ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **optimistic UI**: applying a change on screen before the server answers ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **pessimistic UI**: changing the screen only after the server confirms ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **read-your-writes**: the tab that wrote shows its own change at once ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **cross-tab sync**: telling the app's other tabs about a change, for example with `BroadcastChannel` ([45 §8](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#8-under-the-hood-failure-modes-observability-offline-levels-and-consistency-choices))
- **effect runner**: the part that performs the effects a pure reducer returns (send, save, post) and feeds their answers back as events ([45 Exercise 45.2](../modules/45-frontend-system-design-method.md#ex45-2))

## Module 46

- **case study**: one product screen designed across the browser and the server ([46 §1](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#1-from-method-to-case-the-parts-every-case-study-carries-and-where-each-cases-difficulty-lies))
- **case template**: this module's six parts every case carries, in order: numbers, diagram, contract, state, failure modes, kernel ([46 §1](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#1-from-method-to-case-the-parts-every-case-study-carries-and-where-each-cases-difficulty-lies))
- **assumed numbers**: figures a case picks and labels "assumed", so its choices can be judged against them ([46 §1](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#1-from-method-to-case-the-parts-every-case-study-carries-and-where-each-cases-difficulty-lies))
- **contract (of a case)**: the calls and payloads between browser and server ([46 §1](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#1-from-method-to-case-the-parts-every-case-study-carries-and-where-each-cases-difficulty-lies))
- **kernel**: the smallest code whose correctness a case's design depends on, with tests ([46 §1](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#1-from-method-to-case-the-parts-every-case-study-carries-and-where-each-cases-difficulty-lies))
- **typeahead**: a search box that suggests results while you type ([46 §2](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#2-autocomplete-and-typeahead-search-a-request-policy-a-prefix-cache-ranking-on-the-server-and-an-accessible-combobox))
- **minimum query length**: the shortest normalized input that may be looked up or sent ([46 §2](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#2-autocomplete-and-typeahead-search-a-request-policy-a-prefix-cache-ranking-on-the-server-and-an-accessible-combobox))
- **normalization (of a query)**: turning the input into one key per meaning: trim, lowercase, collapse spaces ([46 §2](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#2-autocomplete-and-typeahead-search-a-request-policy-a-prefix-cache-ranking-on-the-server-and-an-accessible-combobox))
- **prefix cache**: a cache keyed by the normalized query whose complete entries can answer longer queries ([46 §2](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#2-autocomplete-and-typeahead-search-a-request-policy-a-prefix-cache-ranking-on-the-server-and-an-accessible-combobox))
- **complete result / truncated result**: a server answer holding every match, or only the top ones that fit the limit ([46 §2](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#2-autocomplete-and-typeahead-search-a-request-policy-a-prefix-cache-ranking-on-the-server-and-an-accessible-combobox))
- **latest-only**: showing only the answer to the most recent request, never an older one arriving late ([46 §2](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#2-autocomplete-and-typeahead-search-a-request-policy-a-prefix-cache-ranking-on-the-server-and-an-accessible-combobox))
- **active descendant**: the popup option named in `aria-activedescendant` while DOM focus stays on the combobox input ([46 §2](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#2-autocomplete-and-typeahead-search-a-request-policy-a-prefix-cache-ranking-on-the-server-and-an-accessible-combobox))
- **infinite-scroll feed**: a list that loads the next page as the reader nears its end ([46 §3](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#3-an-infinite-scroll-feed-cursor-pages-deduplication-new-items-without-a-jump-and-a-bounded-window))
- **new-items buffer**: live posts held out of the list until the reader asks to see them ([46 §3](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#3-an-infinite-scroll-feed-cursor-pages-deduplication-new-items-without-a-jump-and-a-bounded-window))
- **pill**: the "N new posts" button that shows the buffer ([46 §3](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#3-an-infinite-scroll-feed-cursor-pages-deduplication-new-items-without-a-jump-and-a-bounded-window))
- **cursor page**: a page that continues after the last item seen, so an insert cannot shift it ([46 §3](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#3-an-infinite-scroll-feed-cursor-pages-deduplication-new-items-without-a-jump-and-a-bounded-window))
- **sentinel**: an empty element after the last item whose entering the viewport triggers the next load ([46 §3](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#3-an-infinite-scroll-feed-cursor-pages-deduplication-new-items-without-a-jump-and-a-bounded-window))
- **deduplication**: keeping one item per id when pages and live pushes overlap ([46 §3](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#3-an-infinite-scroll-feed-cursor-pages-deduplication-new-items-without-a-jump-and-a-bounded-window))
- **anchor post**: the id of the post at the top of the viewport, used to restore position ([46 §3](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#3-an-infinite-scroll-feed-cursor-pages-deduplication-new-items-without-a-jump-and-a-bounded-window))
- **server-side filtering**: sending the filters, sort and page size to the server, which returns one page, because the browser holds only that page ([46 §4](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#4-a-large-data-grid-server-side-filtering-sorting-and-pagination-the-query-in-the-url-and-keyset-cursors))
- **canonical query**: one written form of a grid query (filters sorted by name, a sort ending with the unique id, a page size, a cursor) that gives one URL and one cache key ([46 §4](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#4-a-large-data-grid-server-side-filtering-sorting-and-pagination-the-query-in-the-url-and-keyset-cursors))
- **tiebreaker**: a unique key, such as the id, that ends every sort so rows with equal sort values keep one order ([46 §4](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#4-a-large-data-grid-server-side-filtering-sorting-and-pagination-the-query-in-the-url-and-keyset-cursors))
- **keyset cursor**: the last row's sort values and id, so the next page is the rows after that row ([46 §4](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#4-a-large-data-grid-server-side-filtering-sorting-and-pagination-the-query-in-the-url-and-keyset-cursors))
- **estimated total**: a row count the server marks as approximate (`exact: false`) so a slow exact count is not waited for ([46 §4](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#4-a-large-data-grid-server-side-filtering-sorting-and-pagination-the-query-in-the-url-and-keyset-cursors))
- **data grid**: the APG's grid pattern used for tabular data, with one tab stop and arrow-key movement inside it ([46 §4](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#4-a-large-data-grid-server-side-filtering-sorting-and-pagination-the-query-in-the-url-and-keyset-cursors))
- **`aria-sort`**: the ARIA attribute on a column header that tells assistive technology the column's sort state ([46 §4](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#4-a-large-data-grid-server-side-filtering-sorting-and-pagination-the-query-in-the-url-and-keyset-cursors))
- **wizard**: a form split into steps shown one at a time ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **step model**: the data saying which steps exist, which are shown and which are valid ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **visibility rule**: a function of the earlier answers that says whether a step is shown ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **validity rule**: a function of the answers that says whether a step's answers are acceptable ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **conditional step**: a step that has a visibility rule ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **draft**: a wizard's saved answers, kept with a schema version so the form can be resumed ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **schema version**: a number saved with a draft naming the form's shape when it was written ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **migration (of a draft)**: a function turning answers of one schema version into the next ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **landing step**: the first visible step that is not yet valid, where a restored draft opens ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **resume**: opening a saved draft on a later visit or another device ([46 §5](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#5-a-multi-step-form-wizard-with-draft-saving-a-step-model-per-step-validation-and-drafts-that-survive-and-migrate))
- **operations dashboard**: a screen of widgets that each show one live metric ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **widget**: a small view of one metric on a dashboard ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **aggregation window**: the span whose events become one value per widget ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **counter (widget kind)**: a widget kind whose window value is the sum of its events ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **gauge (widget kind)**: a widget kind whose window value is the last event ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **percentile (nearest-rank)**: the value at rank ceil(p × n) of the sorted window; other tools interpolate and differ ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **stale limit**: the silence after which a widget is marked stale while its value stays visible ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **ring buffer**: a fixed-size store that overwrites its oldest point, so memory stays bounded ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **hidden tab**: a page whose document is not visible, such as a background tab, where timers are throttled and frames stop ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **resync**: reloading a snapshot instead of replaying what a throttled client missed ([46 §6](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#6-a-real-time-operations-dashboard-aggregation-per-widget-staleness-hidden-tabs-and-screens-left-open-for-days))
- **notifications center**: the bell and its list of unread items ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **unread set**: the ids of delivered notifications that have no read receipt; the count is derived from it ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **read receipt**: a message saying a notification was read, which may arrive before the notification ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **catch-up**: the events a reconnecting client receives after its last event id, which may repeat some it already has ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **presence**: who is currently active in a document ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **heartbeat (presence)**: a small message an active editor sends so its entry is renewed; not a STOMP heart-beat, which shows a connection is alive ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **time-to-live**: how long an entry survives without a heartbeat before it expires ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **editing indicator**: the display of who is editing, a hint and not a lock ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **lock**: a mechanism that refuses other edits while one user holds it ([46 §7](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#7-a-notifications-center-and-an-editing-indicator-delivery-unread-counts-across-tabs-and-presence-with-a-time-to-live))
- **idempotency key**: a unique value the client sends in the `Idempotency-Key` header so the server recognizes retries of one request; made once per intent, never per attempt ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **intent (transfer)**: one confirmed transfer, its payload and its idempotency key; a changed amount is a new intent ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **double submit**: a second send of the same intent (two clicks, two tabs), which must stay one intent with one key ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **lost response**: a request whose answer never arrived (a timeout, a dropped connection), so the client does not know whether it was applied ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **unknown outcome**: the state after a lost response; not a failure, settled by a retry with the same key or a status read ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **pending (money)**: what the screen shows for a transfer with no final answer: an immediate acknowledgement, never "sent" or a new balance ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **structured-field String**: an RFC 9651 String, written in double quotes; the Idempotency-Key value must be one ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **idempotency fingerprint**: a value the resource derives from the request payload to tell a retry from a different request with the same key (draft-08 §2.4) ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **replay (idempotency)**: answering a completed retry with the stored first response; our server's choice and Stripe API v1's documented behavior, not the draft's requirement ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **status read**: our lookup of a transfer by its idempotency key, used to settle an unknown outcome; the draft defines none ([46 §8](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#8-under-the-hood-a-money-transfer-with-idempotency-keys-optimistic-ui-that-never-claims-success-and-the-unknown-outcome))
- **lease**: a lock's time limit, renewed by its holder, so a holder that vanished stops blocking others when it runs out ([46 Q46.21](../modules/46-system-design-case-studies.md#q46-21))

## Module 47

- **behavioral question**: a question that asks for a past situation, as evidence of how you work ([47 §1](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#1-the-behavioral-round-what-a-story-is-evidence-of-star-with-the-weight-on-action-and-an-explicit-result-and-a-story-bank))
- **STAR**: situation, task, action, result; the shape of a story, with two sentences of situation and task, the action as the substance and the result last ([47 §1](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#1-the-behavioral-round-what-a-story-is-evidence-of-star-with-the-weight-on-action-and-an-explicit-result-and-a-story-bank))
- **result sentence**: our term for a story's last sentence, which states the outcome, with a number and its baseline where one exists, even when the outcome is negative ([47 §1](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#1-the-behavioral-round-what-a-story-is-evidence-of-star-with-the-weight-on-action-and-an-explicit-result-and-a-story-bank))
- **story bank**: our term for six to eight prepared stories, each tagged with the prompts it answers ([47 §1](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#1-the-behavioral-round-what-a-story-is-evidence-of-star-with-the-weight-on-action-and-an-explicit-result-and-a-story-bank))
- **prompt**: the question an interviewer asks that a story answers ([47 §1](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#1-the-behavioral-round-what-a-story-is-evidence-of-star-with-the-weight-on-action-and-an-explicit-result-and-a-story-bank))
- **story skeleton**: our term for the ordered beats one story type needs; section 2 holds the four (performance win, migration, incident, disagreement) ([47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **baseline (measurement)**: the measurement taken before a change, against which the result is stated ([47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **field data**: metrics from real users' browsers, such as RUM or CrUX ([08 §6](../modules/08-browser-rendering-dom-events.md#6-core-web-vitals-lcp-inp-and-cls), [47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **lab measurement**: one controlled run on a stated setup, which explains why but not how bad for whom ([31 §1](../modules/31-performance.md#1-measure-first-the-loop-lab-versus-field-and-what-each-tool-answers), [47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **bottleneck**: the step that limits a metric ([47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **mitigation**: an action that stops users' pain before the cause is known, such as restoring the previous release's files ([47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **root cause**: why an incident happened, found after mitigation ([47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **abandoned hypothesis**: an explanation of an incident that you tested and dropped ([47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **version skew**: one tab's `index.html` and chunks from two releases ([44 §8](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#8-feature-flags-with-openfeature-and-canary-releases-providers-evaluation-context-sticky-rollout-kill-switches-and-version-skew), [47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **commit to a decision**: to build the chosen option well after arguing against it ([47 §2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#2-four-front-end-stories-a-performance-win-a-migration-a-production-incident-and-a-disagreement-over-architecture))
- **legacy code**: our working definition: code that still runs in production and that its current team did not write or cannot change safely ([47 §3](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#3-legacy-codebases-and-migrations-without-blame-the-constraints-of-the-time-how-you-learned-the-code-before-changing-it-and-words-that-explain-instead-of-accuse))
- **blame-free account**: our term for describing old code through the constraints of its time, how you learned it and what you would do differently, without judging people ([47 §3](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#3-legacy-codebases-and-migrations-without-blame-the-constraints-of-the-time-how-you-learned-the-code-before-changing-it-and-words-that-explain-instead-of-accuse))
- **constraint of the time**: a version, tool, deadline or team size that shaped old code ([47 §3](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#3-legacy-codebases-and-migrations-without-blame-the-constraints-of-the-time-how-you-learned-the-code-before-changing-it-and-words-that-explain-instead-of-accuse))
- **characterization test**: a test that pins what existing code does now, quirks included, so a refactor can prove it kept them (Wikipedia, read 2026-10-23) ([47 §3](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#3-legacy-codebases-and-migrations-without-blame-the-constraints-of-the-time-how-you-learned-the-code-before-changing-it-and-words-that-explain-instead-of-accuse))
- **quirk**: behavior that looks wrong but that something may depend on ([47 §3](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#3-legacy-codebases-and-migrations-without-blame-the-constraints-of-the-time-how-you-learned-the-code-before-changing-it-and-words-that-explain-instead-of-accuse))
- **deliberate change**: a change to a pinned quirk made on purpose, as its own visible step ([47 §3](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#3-legacy-codebases-and-migrations-without-blame-the-constraints-of-the-time-how-you-learned-the-code-before-changing-it-and-words-that-explain-instead-of-accuse))
- **live-coding round**: a round in which you build one component live; the round 45 §1 calls machine coding ([47 §4](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#4-live-coding-an-angular-task-clarify-say-the-plan-build-in-working-increments-narrate-and-recover-when-stuck))
- **live-coding loop**: our seven steps for a live-coding round: clarify, say the plan, build in increments, narrate, try edges, check time, wrap up ([47 §4](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#4-live-coding-an-angular-task-clarify-say-the-plan-build-in-working-increments-narrate-and-recover-when-stuck))
- **increment**: one running behavior added to a working version ([47 §4](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#4-live-coding-an-angular-task-clarify-say-the-plan-build-in-working-increments-narrate-and-recover-when-stuck))
- **checkpoint (interview)**: a question to the interviewer at a working state, such as "does this match what you want?" ([47 §4](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#4-live-coding-an-angular-task-clarify-say-the-plan-build-in-working-increments-narrate-and-recover-when-stuck))
- **edge case**: an input at the border of what the code handles, such as an empty result or a page past the end ([47 §4](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#4-live-coding-an-angular-task-clarify-say-the-plan-build-in-working-increments-narrate-and-recover-when-stuck))
- **take-home**: a scoped task done alone and reviewed later, usually in a call ([47 §5](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#5-take-home-assignments-scope-and-time-box-what-the-repository-must-show-the-readme-and-the-review-call))
- **time box**: the agreed time you stop at ([47 §5](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#5-take-home-assignments-scope-and-time-box-what-the-repository-must-show-the-readme-and-the-review-call))
- **definition of done**: what the reviewer will accept as finished ([47 §5](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#5-take-home-assignments-scope-and-time-box-what-the-repository-must-show-the-readme-and-the-review-call))
- **gold-plating**: adding features or polish nobody asked for ([47 §5](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#5-take-home-assignments-scope-and-time-box-what-the-repository-must-show-the-readme-and-the-review-call))
- **commit history**: the sequence of commits, which should tell the story in steps ([47 §5](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#5-take-home-assignments-scope-and-time-box-what-the-repository-must-show-the-readme-and-the-review-call))
- **README**: our suggested file for the reviewer: how to run and test, what is implemented, decisions and trade-offs, what is next, time spent ([47 §5](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#5-take-home-assignments-scope-and-time-box-what-the-repository-must-show-the-readme-and-the-review-call))
- **review call**: the follow-up conversation after a take-home, to walk through a decision and change the code live ([47 §5](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#5-take-home-assignments-scope-and-time-box-what-the-repository-must-show-the-readme-and-the-review-call))
- **candidate's questions**: our term for the questions you ask at the end of a round, to show what you care about and to collect evidence about the team ([47 §6](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#6-questions-to-ask-about-the-front-end-practice-versions-and-updates-testing-the-design-system-and-accessibility-performance-contracts-and-releases-and-how-to-read-the-answers))
- **practice (of a team)**: how a team routinely updates, tests and ships its front end ([47 §6](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#6-questions-to-ask-about-the-front-end-practice-versions-and-updates-testing-the-design-system-and-accessibility-performance-contracts-and-releases-and-how-to-read-the-answers))
- **support window (Angular version)**: how long a major Angular version receives fixes ([39 §3](../modules/39-version-history-and-migrations.md#3-how-angular-ships-semantic-versioning-the-release-cadence-support-windows-and-the-deprecation-policy), [47 §6](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#6-questions-to-ask-about-the-front-end-practice-versions-and-updates-testing-the-design-system-and-accessibility-performance-contracts-and-releases-and-how-to-read-the-answers))
- **real-user monitoring**: field data collected from real visitors' browsers ([44 §9](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#9-frontend-error-tracking-and-real-user-monitoring-releases-source-maps-beacons-and-sampling), [47 §6](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#6-questions-to-ask-about-the-front-end-practice-versions-and-updates-testing-the-design-system-and-accessibility-performance-contracts-and-releases-and-how-to-read-the-answers))
- **design system**: a shared component library with its rules ([36 §12](../modules/36-material-and-cdk.md#12-a-design-system-on-the-cdk-and-angular-aria-or-a-wrapper-around-material), [47 §6](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#6-questions-to-ask-about-the-front-end-practice-versions-and-updates-testing-the-design-system-and-accessibility-performance-contracts-and-releases-and-how-to-read-the-answers))
- **rollback**: returning users to the previous release ([44 §7](../modules/44-fullstack-delivery-and-operations.md#7-cicd-for-both-layers-one-image-promoted-through-stages-a-monorepo-or-separate-repositories-and-versioning-the-contract), [47 §6](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#6-questions-to-ask-about-the-front-end-practice-versions-and-updates-testing-the-design-system-and-accessibility-performance-contracts-and-releases-and-how-to-read-the-answers))
- **follow-up ladder**: our term for six follow-up questions that drill into one story: what you did, how you know, what it cost, what else could explain it, what you would change, what happened after ([47 §7](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#7-under-the-hood-how-one-story-is-drilled-into-numbers-you-can-defend-and-telling-a-design-or-a-migration-as-your-own-project))
- **rung**: one question of the follow-up ladder, asking for more specific evidence than the one above ([47 §7](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#7-under-the-hood-how-one-story-is-drilled-into-numbers-you-can-defend-and-telling-a-design-or-a-migration-as-your-own-project))
- **defensible number**: a number with a source (a dashboard, a report, a test), a baseline and a kind (field or lab) ([47 §7](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#7-under-the-hood-how-one-story-is-drilled-into-numbers-you-can-defend-and-telling-a-design-or-a-migration-as-your-own-project))
- **confounder**: another change that could explain the same result ([47 §7](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#7-under-the-hood-how-one-story-is-drilled-into-numbers-you-can-defend-and-telling-a-design-or-a-migration-as-your-own-project))
- **project story**: our term for a design or migration you took part in, told in STAR with your part as the Action ([47 §7](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#7-under-the-hood-how-one-story-is-drilled-into-numbers-you-can-defend-and-telling-a-design-or-a-migration-as-your-own-project))
- **extract function**: a refactoring step that moves a block of code into a named function without changing what it returns ([Exercise 47.1](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#ex47-1))
- **minor units**: the smallest unit of a currency, such as cents, held as an integer ([Exercise 47.1](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#ex47-1))
- **reducer**: a pure function from a state and an event to the next state; Exercise 47.2's variant b uses one for the list query ([47 Exercise 47.2](../modules/47-behavioral-and-interview-craft.md#ex47-2))
